A
. Observe that

\kappa_{2t}H^{2t}_{\dR}(A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2t})=H^{2t,0}(A^{2t})\oplus H^{0,2t}(A^{2t}).

We realize the constant VHS
\kappa_{2t}H^{2t}(A^{2t},\mathbb{Q})(t)
of weight
0
as arising from the trivial representation
\mathbb{R}^{2}
of
G
with basis
\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
. Mimicking what was done above, an orthonormal basis of
C^{\infty}
-sections is suggestively written as

\displaystyle\mu_{0}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(2\pi i)^{t}}{(2y_{0})^{t}}(\bar{\tau}_{0}e_{1}+e_{2})^{2t}

\displaystyle\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(2\pi i)^{t}}{(2y_{0})^{t}}(\tau_{0}e_{1}+e_{2})^{2t}.

These sections are of type
(-t,t)
and
(t,-t)
respectively. For future use, we let
c:=(2\pi i)^{t}/(2y_{0})^{t}
.

From now on we take
p=k-1
. Then
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}=\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{k,t}=\mathcal{V}_{k-1}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the PVHS defined in (
33
). The
C^{\infty}
-sections

w_{n,j}:=v_{n}\otimes\mu_{j},\qquad 0\leq n\leq 2k-2,\qquad j=0,2t,

form an orthonormal basis of
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}^{\infty}:={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
with respect to the Hermitian form associated to the polarization. Each
w_{n,j}
is of pure type
(n-k+1+j-t,k-1-n+t-j)
. In particular, the
C^{\infty}
-subbundle
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}^{\infty}_{0}
of sections of pure type
(0,0)
has rank
2
with orthonormal basis given by

\{w_{k+t-1,0},w_{k-t-1,2t}\}.

We have

w_{k+t-1,0}=\frac{\binom{2k-2}{k+t-1}^{1/2}(2\pi i)^{k+t-1}}{(2y)^{k-1}(2y_{0})^{t}}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{k+t-1}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{k-t-1}\otimes(\bar{\tau}_{0}e_{1}+e_{2})^{2t}

and

w_{k-t-1,2t}=\frac{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}^{1/2}(2\pi i)^{k+t-1}}{(2y)^{k-1}(2y_{0})^{t}}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{k-t-1}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{k+t-1}\otimes(\tau_{0}e_{1}+e_{2})^{2t}.

Observe in particular that
\overline{w_{k+t-1,0}}=w_{k-t-1,2t}
.

Remark 6.9
.

The
C^{\infty}
-subbundle
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0,\mathbb{R}}
of real
(0,0)
-vectors (considered in Proposition
4.1
) has rank
2
with basis given by
w(+):=w_{p+t,0}+w_{p-t,2t}
and
w(-):=i(w_{p+t,0}-w_{p-t,2t})
. We prefer to work in the
\mathbb{C}
-vector space
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0,\mathbb{R}}\otimes K
, which has basis

(39)

w^{+}(z):=y^{t}w_{k+t-1,0}(z)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad w^{-}(z):=y^{-t}w_{k-t-1,2t}(z),

for
z=x+iy\in\mathcal{H}
.
In this basis, the Laplacian is represented by a diagonal matrix.

7.
Laplacians

We compute the Laplacian associated to the PVHS
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
defined in (
33
) on sections of type
(0,0)
. This will be used to identify the Green’s kernel associated to
\mathcal{W}
in the next section.

7.1.
Hodge star operators

For general background on Hodge operators, see
[
37
, Ch. 5]
.
Let
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\infty}
denote the sheaf of real-valued smooth functions on
\mathcal{H}
and let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}
denote the sheaf of holomorphic functions on
\mathcal{H}
. Let
T_{\mathbb{R}}
denote the real tangent bundle of
\mathcal{H}
and let
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{R}}^{1}
denote the sheaf of real-valued differential
1
-forms on
\mathcal{H}
. If
z\in\mathcal{H}
then by definition the dual vector space
(T_{\mathbb{R},z})^{*}
is equal to the fiber
\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{R},z}
of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{R}}^{1}
at
z
.
Consider the Poincaré metric given for
z=x+iy\in\mathcal{H}
on the tangent space
T_{\mathbb{R},z}
by

g_{z}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1/y^{2}&0\\
0&1/y^{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

This defines a Riemannian metric on the upper half-plane
\mathcal{H}
.
The linear map
r:T_{\mathbb{R},z}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{R},z}
defined by
Y_{z}\mapsto g_{z}(\>\cdot\>,Y_{z})
is an isomorphism. If
\alpha_{z}\in\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{R},z}
, then let
\alpha_{z}^{\#}:=r^{-1}(\alpha_{z})
. We define an inner product on
\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{R},z}
by

\langle\alpha_{z},\beta_{z}\rangle_{z}:=g_{z}(\alpha_{z}^{\#},\beta_{z}^{\#}).

One checks that
dx^{\#}=y^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
and
dy^{\#}=y^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial y}
so that the inner product is given by the matrix
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y^{2}&0\\
0&y^{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Finally, we endow
\bigwedge^{2}\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{R},z}
with the inner product

\langle dx\wedge dy,dx\wedge dy\rangle_{z}=\det\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y^{2}&0\\
0&y^{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=y^{4}.

It follows that the volume form on
\mathcal{H}
associated to the Poincaré metric is given by
\Vol_{z}=\frac{dx\wedge dy}{y^{2}}
.

Suppose that a complex (
C^{\infty}
-bundle)
B
over
\mathcal{H}
is endowed with a positive-definite Hermitian form
\langle\;,\;\rangle
.
Let
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}
denote the sheaf of complex valued differential forms on
\mathcal{H}
of type
(p,q)
. It is equipped with the Hermitian product induced by the Poincaré metric. The sheaf of
B
-valued differential forms
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}(B):=\mathcal{A}^{p,q}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}B
of type
(p,q)
is thus equipped with a positive-definite Hermitian product.
The Hodge star operator for
B
is an anti-linear isomorphism of sheaves

\bar{\ast}_{B}:\mathcal{A}^{p,q}(B)\overset{\sim}{{\longrightarrow\,}}\mathcal{A}^{1-p,1-q}(B^{*})

characterised by the following property: if
\beta\otimes\eta\in\mathcal{A}^{p,q}(B)(\mathcal{H})
, then for all
z\in\mathcal{H}
and all
\alpha_{z}\otimes\omega\in\mathcal{A}^{p,q}(B)_{z}
we have

\alpha_{z}\otimes\omega\wedge(\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}(\beta\otimes\eta))_{z}=\langle\alpha_{z}\otimes\omega,\beta_{z}\otimes\eta\rangle_{z}\Vol_{z}.

From this, we deduce that

(40)

\bar{\ast}_{B}(\beta\otimes\eta)=\overline{\ast\beta}\otimes\eta^{*},

where
\eta^{*}\in\mathcal{W}^{*}
is the form
\eta^{*}=\langle-,\eta\rangle
and

(41)

\ast:\mathcal{A}^{k}_{\mathbb{R}}\overset{\sim}{{\longrightarrow\,}}\mathcal{A}^{2-k}_{\mathbb{R}},\qquad\qquad k=0,1,2,

is the Hodge star operator on
\mathcal{H}
with respect to the Poincaré metric. The latter is a linear bundle isomorphism satisfying

(42)

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\ast dx=dy&&\ast dy=-dx\\
\ast 1=\frac{dx\wedge dy}{y^{2}}&&\ast dx\wedge dy=y^{2}.\end{array}

One checks that
[
25
, bottom of p. 168]

(43)

\bar{\ast}_{B^{*}}\circ\bar{\ast}_{B}=(-1)^{p+q}

on
\mathcal{A}^{p,q}(B)
.

We apply this theory to the complex vector bundle
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}^{\infty}
(
33
) endowed with the Hermitian pairing given on fibers over
z\in\mathcal{H}
by

\langle(2\pi i)^{p}v_{1}\ldots v_{2p}\otimes(2\pi i)^{t}\omega_{1},(2\pi i)^{p}w_{1}\ldots w_{2p}\otimes(2\pi i)^{t}\omega_{2}\rangle_{z}\\
=\langle(2\pi i)^{p}v_{1}\ldots v_{2p},(2\pi i)^{p}w_{1}\ldots w_{2p}\rangle_{2p,z}^{0}\langle(2\pi i)^{t}\omega_{1},(2\pi i)^{t}\omega_{2}\rangle_{2t,\tau_{0}}^{0}.

The associated Hodge star operator will be denoted by
\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}:=\bar{\ast}_{\tilde{\mathcal{W}}^{\infty}}
. The one for the dual bundle will similarly be denoted by
\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}
.

7.2.
Gauss–Manin connections

The holomorphic complex vector bundle
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\tilde{W}_{\mathbb{C}}
(
33
) comes equipped with the Gauss–Manin connection

\tilde{\nabla}:=\partial\otimes 1\colon\tilde{\mathcal{W}}{\longrightarrow\,}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}

satisfying Griffiths transversality

\tilde{\nabla}(\Fil^{p})\subset\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\Fil^{p-1}.

Notation 7.1
.

In order to make the notation less cumbersome, we drop the tilde in the notations for the bundles and connections in this subsection and the next, and remember that we are working over
\bigsqcup\mathcal{H}
.

We consider the complex
C^{\infty}
-bundle
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}:={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}W_{\mathbb{C}}
and extend the Gauss–Manin connection to
D:=d\otimes 1\colon\mathcal{W}^{\infty}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{H}}}\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
. With the description
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\mathcal{W}
, we have

D=\bar{\partial}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla=\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{W}}+1\otimes\nabla.

(While the operator
d\otimes 1
is not defined on the holomorphic vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
, the operator
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{W}}:=\bar{\partial}\otimes 1
is on the other hand well-defined.)
The pairing
(\>,\>)=(\>,\>)_{k,t}^{0}
(
34
) inducing the structure of PVHS is flat, or horizontal, in the sense of Remark
6.4
:

(44)

d(v,\bar{w})=(D(v),\bar{w})+(v,\overline{D(w)}).

In other words,
D
is a Hermitian connection in the sense of
[
25
, Def. 4.2.9]
. It is important to note here that if
\alpha\otimes s\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(\mathcal{W}^{\infty})
and
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
, then
(\alpha\otimes s,s^{\prime})=\alpha(s,s^{\prime})
.
In Zucker’s notations
[
42
, p. 423]
, define the pairing
((v,w))=(v,\bar{w})
. Then (
44
) becomes

(45)

d((v,w))=((D(v),w))+((v,D(w))).

Let
C_{\mathcal{W}}
denote the Weil operator of
\mathcal{W}
given by the direct sum of the scalar operators
i^{p-q}
on
\mathcal{W}^{p,q}
. Then the associated Hermitian pairing can be expressed as

\langle v,w\rangle=((C_{\mathcal{W}}v,w))=((v,C_{\mathcal{W}}w)).

On
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}W_{\mathbb{C}}
, we have

D=\partial\otimes 1+\bar{\partial}\otimes 1
, and we observe by Griffiths transversality that

D(\mathcal{A}^{r,s}(\Fil^{p}))\subset\mathcal{A}^{r+1,s}(\Fil^{p-1})\oplus\mathcal{A}^{r,s+1}(\Fil^{p}).

The complex
C^{\infty}
-bundle
\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}
decomposes as
\mathcal{A}^{1,0}\oplus\mathcal{A}^{0,1}
. Similarly, the PVHS
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
of weight
0
admits a Hodge decomposition
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}=\oplus_{k}\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}
. Following
[
42
, (1.8)]
, we have

D(\mathcal{A}^{r,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}))\subset\mathcal{A}^{r+1,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k})\oplus\mathcal{A}^{r+1,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k-1,-k+1})\oplus\mathcal{A}^{r,s+1}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k})\oplus\mathcal{A}^{r,s+1}(\mathcal{W}^{k+1,-k-1}),

and the Gauss–Manin connection
D
splits into
4
components:

\displaystyle\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{A}^{r,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}){\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{r+1,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k})

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{A}^{r,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}){\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{r,s+1}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k})

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{A}^{r,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}){\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{r+1,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k-1,-k+1})

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{A}^{r,s}(\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}){\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{r,s+1}(\mathcal{W}^{k+1,-k-1}).

Lemma 7.1
.

If
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}^{r,s}
and
s\in\mathcal{W}^{k,-k}
, then

\displaystyle\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(\alpha\otimes s)

\displaystyle=\partial(\alpha)\otimes s+(-1)^{r+s}\alpha\wedge\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(s)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(\alpha\otimes s)

\displaystyle=\bar{\partial}(\alpha)\otimes s+(-1)^{r+s}\alpha\wedge\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(s)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(\alpha\otimes s)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r+s}\alpha\wedge\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(s)

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(\alpha\otimes s)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r+s}\alpha\wedge\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(s).

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the connection is extended to
D\colon\mathcal{A}^{n}(\mathcal{W}){\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{n+1}(\mathcal{W})
via the Leibniz rule
D(\beta\otimes s)=d\beta\otimes s+(-1)^{n}\beta\wedge D(s)
.
∎

The Hermitian metric on
\mathcal{W}
induces an anti-linear isomorphism

(46)

(\;)^{*}\colon\mathcal{W}\overset{\sim}{{\longrightarrow\,}}\mathcal{W}^{*},\qquad w{\longrightarrow\,}w^{*}:=\langle-,w\rangle.

Since the basis
\{w_{n,j}
,
0\leq n\leq 2p
,
j=0,2t\}
is orthonormal with respect to the Hermitian pairing, the dual basis
\{w_{n,j}^{\vee}
,
0\leq n\leq 2p
,
j=0,2t\}
satisfies
w_{n,j}^{\vee}=\langle-,w_{n,j}\rangle=w_{n,j}^{*}
.
We extend (
46
) to an anti-linear isomorphism

(47)

(\;)^{*}\colon\mathcal{A}^{p,q}(\mathcal{W})\overset{\sim}{{\longrightarrow\,}}\mathcal{A}^{q,p}(\mathcal{W}^{*})

by declaring that if
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}^{p,q}
and
s\in\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{W})
, then

(\alpha\otimes s)^{*}:=\bar{\alpha}\otimes s^{*}.

The complex dual
C^{\infty}
-bundle
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\infty}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}}\mathcal{W}^{*}
has an integral connection
D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}
defined by

D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(f)(s)=d(f(s))-f(D(s)).

If
D(s)=\alpha\otimes t\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(\mathcal{W})
with
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
t\in\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{W})
, then
f(D(s)):=\alpha f(t)
.
As above, this connection splits into four components
\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}},\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}},\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}},
and
\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}
. We extend the connection to
D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\colon\mathcal{A}^{n}(\mathcal{W}^{*}){\longrightarrow\,}\mathcal{A}^{n+1}(\mathcal{W}^{*})
via the usual Leibniz rule
D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(\beta\otimes t)=d\beta\otimes t+(-1)^{n}\beta\wedge D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(t)
.

Lemma 7.2
.

For all
A\in\mathcal{A}^{p}(\mathcal{W})
and
B\in\mathcal{A}^{q}(\mathcal{W}^{*})
,
the following Leibniz rule holds:

d(A\wedge B)=D_{\mathcal{W}}(A)\wedge B+(-1)^{p}A\wedge D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(B).

Proof.

Writing
A=\alpha\otimes s\in\mathcal{A}^{p}(\mathcal{W})
and
B=\beta\otimes t\in\mathcal{A}^{q}(\mathcal{W}^{*})
, we must prove the following equality:

(48)

d((\alpha\otimes s)\wedge(\beta\otimes t))=D_{\mathcal{W}}(\alpha\otimes s)\wedge(\beta\otimes t)+(-1)^{p}(\alpha\otimes s)\wedge D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(\beta\otimes t).

By definition, we have

D_{\mathcal{W}}(\alpha\otimes s)=d\alpha\otimes s+(-1)^{p}\alpha\wedge D_{\mathcal{W}}(s)

and

D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(\beta\otimes t)=d\beta\otimes t+(-1)^{q}\beta\wedge D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(t).

The proof then follows from an easy calculation using the Leibniz rule for the exterior derivative and the definition of the connection
D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}
.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

For all
w\in\mathcal{W}
, we have

D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(w^{*})=(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}D_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w))^{*}.

Proof.

For all
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(w^{*})(w^{\prime})

\displaystyle=d(w^{*}(w^{\prime}))-w^{*}(D_{\mathcal{W}}(w^{\prime}))=d\langle w^{\prime},w\rangle-\langle D_{\mathcal{W}}(w^{\prime}),w\rangle

\displaystyle=d((w^{\prime},C_{\mathcal{W}}w))-((D_{\mathcal{W}}(w^{\prime}),C_{\mathcal{W}}w))=((w^{\prime},D_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w)))

\displaystyle=((w^{\prime},C_{\mathcal{W}}C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}D_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w)))=\langle w^{\prime},C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}D_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w)\rangle=(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}D_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w))^{*}(w^{\prime}),

where we used (
45
) in the fourth equality.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

For all
w\in\mathcal{W}
, we have

\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(w^{*})=(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w))^{*}\quad\text{ and }\quad\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(w^{*})=(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w))^{*},

\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(w^{*})=(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w))^{*}\quad\text{ and }\quad\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}(w^{*})=(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w))^{*}.

Proof.

Immediate consequence of Lemma
7.3
after identifying the four components.
∎

Let
p=k-1
and recall the sections

(49)

w^{-}(z)=y^{-t}w_{p-t,2t}(z)\quad\text{ and }\quad w^{+}(z)=y^{t}w_{p+t,0}(z)

(with
z=x+iy\in\mathcal{H}
) introduced in Remark
6.9
.
For each
0\leq n\leq 2p
, define

\xi_{n}=\left(\binom{2p}{n}^{1/2}\frac{(2\pi i)^{p}}{2^{p}}\right)^{-1},

so that

\xi_{p+t}w^{+}=y^{t-p}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}\otimes\mu_{0}

and

\xi_{p-t}w^{-}=y^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}\otimes\mu_{2t}.

In what follows, the calculations will mostly be carried out for
w^{-}
. The ones for
w^{+}
can be verified similarly. (We also implicitly work in the complex vector space
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0,\mathbb{R}}\otimes K
, as in Remark
6.9
.)

Lemma 7.5
.

Given
F\in\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\mathcal{H})
, we have

\displaystyle\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}-\frac{2tF}{z-\bar{z}}\right)dz\otimes w^{-}

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}d\bar{z}\otimes w^{-}

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=-\frac{p-t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-t}\xi_{p-t-1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes w_{p-t-1,2t}

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}d\bar{z}\otimes w_{p-t+1,2t}.

Proof.

We will often use the identity
2iy=z-\bar{z}
, from which it follows that

\frac{\partial y}{\partial z}=\frac{1}{2i}\quad\text{ and }\quad\frac{\partial y}{\partial\bar{z}}=-\frac{1}{2i}.

Using
D=d\otimes 1=\partial\otimes 1+\bar{\partial}\otimes 1
on
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}W_{\mathbb{C}}
, we begin by computing

\displaystyle(\partial\otimes 1)(\xi_{p-t}Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}y^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}dz\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle-\frac{p+t}{2i}Fy^{-p-t-1}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}dz\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle+(p-t)u_{1}Fy^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t-1}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}dz\otimes\mu_{2t}.

From
(z-\bar{z})u_{1}=(zu_{1}+u_{2})-(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\partial\otimes 1)(\xi_{p-t}Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}-\frac{2tF}{z-\bar{z}}\right)y^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}dz\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle-\frac{p-t}{(z-\bar{z})}Fy^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t-1}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t+1}dz\otimes\mu_{2t}.

Hence,

\displaystyle(\partial\otimes 1)(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}-\frac{2tF}{z-\bar{z}}\right)dz\otimes w^{-}-\frac{p-t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-t}\xi_{p-t-1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes w_{p-t-1,2t},

and we conclude by separating terms in
\mathcal{A}^{1,0}(\mathcal{W}^{0,0})
and
\mathcal{A}^{1,0}(\mathcal{W}^{-1,1})
.

On the other hand, we compute

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}\otimes 1)(\xi_{p-t}Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}y^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}d\bar{z}\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle+\frac{p+t}{2i}Fy^{-p-t-1}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}d\bar{z}\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle+(p+t)u_{1}Fy^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t-1}d\bar{z}\otimes\mu_{2t}.

Using the fact that
(z-\bar{z})u_{1}=(zu_{1}+u_{2})-(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}\otimes 1)(\xi_{p-t}Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}y^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}d\bar{z}\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle+\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-p-t}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t+1}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t-1}d\bar{z}\otimes\mu_{2t}.

Hence,

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}\otimes 1)(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}d\bar{z}\otimes w^{-}+\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}d\bar{z}\otimes w_{p-t+1,2t},

and we conclude by separating terms in
\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\mathcal{W}^{0,0})
and
\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\mathcal{W}^{1,-1})
.
∎

7.3.
Computing Laplacians

Definition 7.6
.

The Laplacian associated to the complex
C^{\infty}
-bundle
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
with connection
D
is

\square_{D}:=DD^{*}+D^{*}D,

where

D^{*}:=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}

denotes the formal adjoint operator with respect to the scalar product on
\mathcal{A}_{c}^{p,q}(\mathcal{W})

(\alpha,\beta)_{L^{2}}:=\int_{\mathcal{H}}\alpha\wedge\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}\beta=\int_{\mathcal{H}}\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle\Vol.

Remark 7.7
.

For the claim that
D^{*}
is indeed the formal adjoint of
D
, see
[
25
, Lem. 4.1.12]
.

Observe that on
\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\mathcal{W})
we have

\square_{D}=D^{*}D,

simply because the adjoint operator on
\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\mathcal{W})
is trivial (as there are no forms of negative type).

Remark 7.8
.

Define
D^{\prime}:=\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}+\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}
and
D^{\prime\prime}:=\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}+\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}
.
Letting

(D^{\prime})^{*}=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad(D^{\prime\prime})^{*}=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D^{\prime\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}

denote the formal adjoints of
D^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime\prime}
and defining Laplacians
\square_{D^{\prime}}
and
\square_{D^{\prime\prime}}
as in Definition
7.6
, we have
[
42
, Thm. 2.7]

(50)

\square_{D}=2\square_{D^{\prime}}=2\square_{D^{\prime\prime}}.

In practice, we will compute
\square_{D^{\prime}}=(D^{\prime})^{*}D^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\mathcal{W})
.

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
w^{+}
and
w^{-}
be the sections defined by (
49
).
Given
F\in\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\mathcal{H})
, we have

\square_{D}(F\cdot w^{-})=\left(-4y^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}-4ity\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}+(k-t-1)(k+t)\right)F\cdot w^{-}

and

\square_{D}(F\cdot w^{+})=\left(-4y^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+4ity\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}+(k+t-1)(k-t)\right)F\cdot w^{+}.

Proof.

During the course of this proof, we set
p=k-1
.
We begin by computing the
(0,0)
-component of
(D^{\prime})^{*}\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})
, using Lemma
7.5
:

\displaystyle(D^{\prime})^{*}

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}-\frac{2tF}{z-\bar{z}}\right)dz\otimes w^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}-\frac{2tF}{z-\bar{z}}\right)dz\otimes w^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\left(i\left(\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t\bar{F}}{z-\bar{z}}\right)d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ(\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}+\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}})\left(i\left(\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t\bar{F}}{z-\bar{z}}\right)d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\left(i\left(\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t\bar{F}}{z-\bar{z}}\right)d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}\right).

Using Lemma
7.1
followed by Lemma
7.4
, we have

\displaystyle\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}

\displaystyle\left(i\left(\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t\bar{F}}{z-\bar{z}}\right)d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial\left(i\left(\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t\bar{F}}{z-\bar{z}}\right)d\bar{z}\right)\otimes(w^{-})^{*}-i\left(\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t\bar{F}}{z-\bar{z}}\right)d\bar{z}\wedge(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w^{-}))^{*}

\displaystyle=i\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\bar{F}}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t}{z-\bar{z}}\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial z}-\frac{2t\bar{F}}{(z-\bar{z})^{2}}\right)dz\wedge d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*},

where in the last equality we used the equality
\bar{\partial}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(w^{-})=0
which follows from Lemma
7.5
.

We deduce that

\displaystyle(D^{\prime})^{*}\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\left(i\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\bar{F}}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+\frac{2t}{z-\bar{z}}\frac{\partial\bar{F}}{\partial z}-\frac{2t\bar{F}}{(z-\bar{z})^{2}}\right)dz\wedge d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(-i\left(\frac{\partial^{2}F}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}-\frac{2t}{z-\bar{z}}\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}-\frac{2tF}{(z-\bar{z})^{2}}\right)2iy^{2}w^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=i\left(\frac{\partial^{2}F}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}-\frac{2t}{z-\bar{z}}\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}-\frac{2tF}{(z-\bar{z})^{2}}\right)2iy^{2}w^{-}

\displaystyle=-\left(2y^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}F}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+2ity\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}+tF\right)w^{-}.

Next, we compute the
(0,0)
-component of
(D^{\prime})^{*}\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})
, using Lemma
7.5
:

\displaystyle(D^{\prime})^{*}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}d\bar{z}\otimes w_{p-t+1,2t}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}}\left(\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}Fy^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}d\bar{z}\otimes w_{p-t+1,2t}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ D^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes(w_{p-t+1,2t})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ(\partial^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}+\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}})\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes(w_{p-t+1,2t})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\circ\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes(w_{p-t+1,2t})^{*}\right).

Using Lemma
7.1
followed by Lemma
7.4
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}

\displaystyle\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes(w_{p-t+1,2t})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\wedge\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}((w_{p-t+1,2t})^{*})\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\wedge(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(C_{\mathcal{W}}w_{p-t+1,2t}))^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\wedge(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(-\xi_{p-t+1}w_{p-t+1,2t}))^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\wedge(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(\xi_{p-t+1}w_{p-t+1,2t}))^{*}.

We have

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle(\xi_{p-t+1}w_{p-t+1,2t})=\nabla^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(y^{-p}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t+1}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t-1}\otimes\mu_{2t})

\displaystyle=-\frac{(p-t+1)}{z-\bar{z}}y^{-p}(zu_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{z}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}dz\otimes\mu_{2t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(p-t+1)}{z-\bar{z}}y^{t}\xi_{p-t}dz\otimes w^{-}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}

\displaystyle\left(i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t+1}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\otimes(w_{p-t+1,2t})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=i\frac{p+t}{z-\bar{z}}\bar{F}y^{-t}\xi_{p-t}^{-1}dz\wedge\left(C_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}\left(-\frac{(p-t+1)}{z-\bar{z}}y^{t}\xi_{p-t}dz\otimes w^{-}\right)\right)^{*}

\displaystyle=i\frac{(p+t)(p-t+1)}{(z-\bar{z})^{2}}\bar{F}dz\wedge d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle(D^{\prime})^{*}

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{W}}(Fw^{-})=-\bar{\ast}_{\mathcal{W}^{*}}\left(i\frac{(p+t)(p-t+1)}{(z-\bar{z})^{2}}\bar{F}dz\wedge d\bar{z}\otimes(w^{-})^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(-\frac{(p+t)(p-t+1)}{2}Fw^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(p+t)(p-t+1)}{2}Fw^{-}.

Putting everything together yields

\displaystyle\square_{D^{\prime}}(Fw^{-})

\displaystyle=-\left(2y^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}F}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+2ity\frac{\partial F}{\partial\bar{z}}+tF\right)w^{-}+\frac{(p+t)(p-t+1)}{2}Fw^{-}

\displaystyle=-\left(2y^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+2ity\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}+t-\frac{(p+t)(p-t+1)}{2}\right)Fw^{-}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(4y^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z\partial\bar{z}}+4ity\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}-(p-t)(p+t+1)\right)Fw^{-}.

We conclude by using (
50
) and recalling that
p=k-1
.
A similar calculation yields the result for
w^{+}
(replace
-t
by
+t
in all calculations).
∎

8.
Green’s kernels

Recall that
C=Y(M)_{\mathbb{C}}
.
In this section,
we construct the Green’s kernel associated to the PVHS
\mathcal{W}
(Proposition
4.1
). More precisely, we construct a harmonic
C^{\infty}
-section of the rank
4
vector bundle
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
over the complement of the diagonal in
\bigsqcup\mathcal{H}\times\bigsqcup\mathcal{H}
that descends to a section of the corresponding bundle over
C\times C
minus the diagonal and admits an extension to a
C^{\infty}
-section of
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
over the complement of the diagonal in
\bar{C}\times\bar{C}
. We begin by analyzing the action of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
on sections of
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
.

8.1.
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-invariant sections

Let
G
be a section of
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
over
\bigsqcup(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
, where we use
\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
to denote the diagonal of
\mathcal{H}
. At a point
(z,z^{\prime})\in(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
,
G(z,z^{\prime})
is an element in the fiber
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})_{(z,z^{\prime})}=\Hom((\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})_{z},(\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})_{z^{\prime}})
.
The bundle
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
has rank
2
with basis
\{w^{+},w^{-}\}
defined by (
49
), so all we need to do to specify a section
G
is to define
G(z,z^{\prime})(w^{-}(z))
and
G(z,z^{\prime})(w^{+}(z))
in terms of
w^{-}(z^{\prime})
and
w^{+}(z^{\prime})
.

Throughout this section, we set
p=k-1
.
Let
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. We have
w^{\pm}(z)\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}=\tilde{W}=\sym^{2p}(V)(p)\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2}
and thus
\gamma
acts (on the left) on
w^{\pm}(z)
via the symmetric standard representation of
G={\rm GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
as follows:

(51)

\gamma\cdot w^{\pm}(z):=\xi_{p\pm t}^{-1}y^{-p\pm t}((az+b)u_{1}+(cz+d)u_{2})^{p\pm t}((a\bar{z}+b)u_{1}+(c\bar{z}+d)u_{2})^{p\mp t}\otimes\mu_{t\mp t}.

In particular, we see that

(52)

\gamma\cdot w^{\pm}(z)=\det(\gamma)^{p\mp t}j(\gamma,z)^{\pm 2t}w^{\pm}(\gamma z).

The left action of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\times{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
on a section
G
of
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
is defined by

(53)

((\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\cdot G)(z,z^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z)):=\gamma^{\prime}\cdot G(\gamma^{-1}z,(\gamma^{\prime})^{-1}z^{\prime})(\gamma^{-1}\cdot w^{\pm}(z)).

In particular, the left diagonal
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-action is given by

(54)

(\gamma\cdot G)(z,z^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z)):=\gamma\cdot G(\gamma^{-1}z,\gamma^{-1}z^{\prime})(\gamma^{-1}\cdot w^{\pm}(z)).

We compute this diagonal action on the section
G_{0}
defined by
G_{0}(z,z^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z))=w^{\pm}(z^{\prime})
:

(\gamma\cdot G_{0})(z,z^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z))=j(\gamma^{-1},z)^{\pm 2t}j(\gamma,\gamma^{-1}z^{\prime})^{\pm 2t}w^{\pm}(z^{\prime}).

With these formulas in hand, it is not difficult to create diagonal
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-invariant sections, as we will now explain.

Consider the function
g=g_{k,t}
on
(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
given by

(55)

g(z,z^{\prime}):=-Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2yy^{\prime}}\right),

where
Q_{k,t}(x)
is the Jacobi function of the second kind (
17
) which we recall is defined, for all
x
in the complex plane cut along the segment
[-1,1]
, by

(56)

Q_{k,t}(x):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{2^{2t}dw}{(x+\sqrt{x^{2}-1}\cosh(w))^{k-t}(x+1+\sqrt{x^{2}-1}e^{w})^{2t}}.

Note that
g
is a function on
(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
that only depends on the hyperbolic distance between
z
and
z^{\prime}
. It follows easily that
g
is invariant under the diagonal action of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, i.e.,
g(\gamma z,\gamma z^{\prime})=g(z,z^{\prime})
for all
\gamma\in{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Define the following functions on
(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
:

\mu_{g}^{+}(z,z^{\prime}):=g(z,z^{\prime})\left(\frac{z-\bar{z}^{\prime}}{2iy^{\prime}}\right)^{2t}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mu_{g}^{-}(z,z^{\prime}):=g(z,z^{\prime})\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}.

A quick calculation reveals that

\mu_{g}^{\pm}(\gamma z,\gamma z^{\prime})=j(\gamma,z)^{\mp 2t}j(\gamma,z^{\prime})^{\pm 2t}\mu_{g}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime}).

Lemma 8.1
.

The section
G_{g}
of
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
defined by

G_{g}(z,z^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z))=\mu_{g}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime})w^{\pm}(z^{\prime}),

is invariant under the left diagonal action (
54
) of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

An easy verification using the fact that for any
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
we have
j(\gamma^{-1},z^{\prime})j(\gamma,\gamma^{-1}z^{\prime})=1
.
∎

8.2.
The Green’s kernel

Now that we have an
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-invariant section
G_{g}
, we will use it to define a section
G_{g,M}
of the bundle
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0})
on
(C\times C)\setminus\Delta_{C}
,
where
\mathcal{W}
denotes the bundle (
32
) on
C
and
\Delta_{C}
is the diagonal in
C\times C
.

By definition, we have a Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigsqcup\mathcal{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(\mathbb{C}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr}

If
z\in\mathcal{H}
and
Q=\pr(z)=\Gamma(M)z\in C(\mathbb{C})
, then the fibers
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}
and
\mathcal{W}_{Q}
are canonically identified since the diagram is Cartesian. Suppose that
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
is another point such that
\pr(z^{\prime})=Q
. Then there exists
\gamma\in\Gamma(M)
such that
\gamma z^{\prime}=z
. The action of
\gamma
on fibers then identifies
\gamma\cdot\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z^{\prime}}
with
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}
.

In order to define a section
G_{g,M}
of
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0})
, it suffices to specify
G_{g,M}(Q,Q^{\prime})\in\Hom(\mathcal{W}_{Q},\mathcal{W}_{Q^{\prime}})
for all
Q\neq Q^{\prime}
in
C(\mathbb{C})
. For this, it is in turn enough to specify
G_{g,M}(z,z^{\prime})\in\Hom((\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})_{z},(\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{0})_{z^{\prime}})
that are independent of choices of representatives
z
and
z^{\prime}
for
Q=\Gamma(M)z
and
Q^{\prime}=\Gamma(M)z^{\prime}
. This last condition is equivalent to requiring that
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\cdot G_{g,M}=G_{g,M}
for all
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma(M)
, where the action is the one defined in (
53
).

Lemma 8.2
.

For all
0<t\leq k-1
, the sum

G=G_{g,M}:=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}(1,\gamma^{-1})\cdot G_{g}

converges uniformly on compact subsets of
\mathcal{H}^{2}\setminus\{(z,z^{\prime})\mid z\in\Gamma(M)z^{\prime}\}
and gives a well-defined section of the bundle
\Hom(\pr_{1}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0},\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{W}_{0})
on
(C\times C)\setminus\Delta_{C}
.

Remark 8.3
.

Assuming the convergence of the sum (to be proved below), we explicitly have, for
Q=\Gamma(M)z\neq\Gamma(M)z^{\prime}=Q^{\prime}
,

(57)

G_{g,M}(Q,Q^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z))=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z^{\prime})^{\mp 2t}\mu^{\pm}_{g}(z,\gamma z^{\prime})w^{\pm}(z^{\prime}).

Using the diagonal
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-invariance of
G_{g}
, we obtain

G_{g,M}=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}(1,\gamma^{-1})\cdot G_{g}=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}(1,\gamma^{-1})\cdot(\gamma\cdot G_{g})=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}(\gamma,1)\cdot G_{g},

which leads to the useful formula

(58)

G_{g,M}(Q,Q^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z))=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z)^{\pm 2t}\mu^{\pm}_{g}(\gamma z,z^{\prime})w^{\pm}(z^{\prime}).

Proof of Lemma
8.2
.

Assuming the convergence of the sum, the
\Gamma(M)
-invariance is an easy verification using the diagonal
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-invariance of
G_{g}
. We focus on the proof of convergence, inspired by the proof of
[
23
, VI Prop. 6.2]
. Let
Q=\Gamma(M)z\neq\Gamma(M)z^{\prime}=Q^{\prime}
.
In view of Remark
8.3
, it suffices to check that the sums

(59)

\mathcal{G}^{+}(z,z^{\prime}):=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z^{\prime})^{-2t}\mu^{+}_{g}(z,\gamma z^{\prime})\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime}):=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z)^{-2t}\mu^{-}_{g}(\gamma z,z^{\prime})

converge uniformly for
(z,z^{\prime})\in E_{1}\times E_{2}
, where
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are compact subsets of
\mathcal{H}
. We have

\mathcal{G}^{+}(z,z^{\prime})=-\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{(z-\gamma\bar{z}^{\prime})}{j(\gamma,z^{\prime})2i\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}\right)^{2t}Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|z-\gamma z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2y\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}\right)\\
=-\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{j(\gamma,\bar{z}^{\prime})(z-\gamma\bar{z}^{\prime})}{2iy^{\prime}}\right)^{2t}Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|z-\gamma z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2y\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}\right).

Using Lemma
2.4
, this is

\displaystyle O

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{|j(\gamma,z^{\prime})||z-\gamma\bar{z}^{\prime}|}{y^{\prime}}\right)^{2t}\left(\frac{2y\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}{|z-\gamma z^{\prime}|^{2}}\right)^{k+t}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\frac{|j(\gamma,z^{\prime})|^{2t}|z-\gamma\bar{z}^{\prime}|^{2t}}{(y^{\prime})^{t}}\left(\frac{2y\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}{|z-\gamma z^{\prime}|^{2}}\right)^{k+t}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{|z-\gamma\bar{z}^{\prime}|^{2}}{\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}\right)^{t}\left(\frac{2y\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}{|z-\gamma z^{\prime}|^{2}}\right)^{k+t}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}{|z-\gamma z^{\prime}|^{2}}\right)^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})}{(1+|\gamma z^{\prime}|)^{2}}\right)^{k}\right),

where the implicit constants depend on
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
. Similarly, it can be shown that

(60)

\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime})=O\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{\Im(\gamma z)}{(1+|\gamma z|)^{2}}\right)^{k}\right).

Since
k\geq 2
, we may apply
[
23
, VI Prop. 5.1]
, which says that for each
\xi\in\mathcal{H}
, we have

\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}\left(\frac{\Im(\gamma\xi)}{(1+|\gamma\xi|)^{2}}\right)^{k}\leq C(k,M),

where
C(k,M)
is some positive constant that only depends on
k
and
M
. The uniform convergence follows as in the proof of
[
23
, VI Prop. 6.2]
.
∎

We may use the basis
\{w^{+},w^{-}\}
to trivialize
\mathcal{W}_{0}
, but this does not extend to a
C^{\infty}
-trivialization of
\bar{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
, because
w^{\pm}
have singularities of type
y^{k\mp t-1}
near the cusp
\infty
(hence singularities of type
\Im(\gamma z)^{{k\mp t-1}}
near the cusp
\gamma\infty
).
Recall from (
59
) the functions
\mathcal{G}^{\pm}
on
\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H}\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
. Using the trivialization
\{w^{+},w^{-}\}
, the section
G
is given by the matrix

\left(\begin{matrix}\mathcal{G}^{+}&0\\
0&\mathcal{G}^{-}\end{matrix}\right).

The remaining part of this section is dedicated to proving that
G
defined in Lemma
8.2
is the Green’s kernel associated to the bundle
\mathcal{W}
on
C
, i.e., that it satisfies the following properties (see Proposition
4.1
):

(i)

G(Q,Q^{\prime})-\log|z(Q)-z(Q^{\prime})|
is bounded near any point
(Q,Q)\in\Delta_{\bar{C}}
, if
z
is a local coordinate on
\bar{C}
near
Q
;

(ii)

G
is harmonic with respect to the Laplacian
\square_{D}
attached to
\mathcal{W}
in the second variable;

(iii)

As
z^{\prime}
approaches the cusp
\infty
, the functions
\langle\Im(\gamma z)^{{k\mp t-1}}G(z,\gamma z^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z)),w^{\pm}(\gamma z^{\prime})\rangle
are
C^{\infty}
for
z^{\prime}
in a neighborhood of
\infty
, for all
\gamma\in\Gamma(M)
.

Proposition 8.4
.

G(Q,Q^{\prime})-\log|z(Q)-z(Q^{\prime})|
is bounded near any point
(Q,Q)\in\Delta_{\bar{C}}
, if
z
is a local coordinate on
\bar{C}
near
Q
.

Proof.

After trivializing the bundle using
w^{+}
and
w^{-}
, it suffices to show that

\mu^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime})-\log|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}

is bounded as
z^{\prime}\to z
in
\mathcal{H}
. This follows from Lemma
2.5
and the fact that
\lim\limits_{z^{\prime}\to z}(\bar{z}-z^{\prime})=-2iy
.
∎

Proposition 8.5
.

The section
G
is harmonic with respect to the Laplacian associated to
\mathcal{W}
in the second variable.

Proof.

Given
(z,z^{\prime})\in(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\Delta_{\mathcal{H}}
, we define

s(z,z^{\prime}):=1+\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2yy^{\prime}}.

Then
g(z,z^{\prime})=-Q_{k,t}(s(z,z^{\prime}))
and by (
58
), for
Q=\Gamma(M)z\neq\Gamma(M)z^{\prime}=Q^{\prime}
, we have

(61)

G(Q,Q^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z))=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z)^{\pm 2t}\mu^{\pm}_{g}(\gamma z,z^{\prime})w^{\pm}(z^{\prime}).

We write
\square_{2}
for the Laplacian
\square_{D}
associated to
\mathcal{W}
in the second variable. Then

\square_{2}(G(Q,Q^{\prime})(w^{\pm}(z)))=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z)^{\pm 2t}\square_{2}(\mu_{g}^{\pm}(\gamma z,z^{\prime})w^{\pm}(z^{\prime})).

In order to prove the proposition, it therefore suffices to prove that

\square_{2}(\mu_{g}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime})w^{\pm}(z^{\prime}))=0

for any
z\in\mathcal{H}
,
z\neq z^{\prime}
.

Define

(62)

\Delta:=-4(y^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z^{\prime}\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}},\quad\Delta_{\pm t}:=\Delta\pm 4ity^{\prime}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda_{\pm t}=-(k\pm t-1)(k\mp t).

By Proposition
7.9
, it suffices to prove that

(63)

\Delta_{\pm t}(\mu_{g}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime}))=\lambda_{\pm t}\mu_{g}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime}).

In what follows, we will write
s
for
s(z,z^{\prime})
to ease the notation.
The following identities are quickly verified:

(64)

\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}=\frac{z^{\prime}-z}{2yy^{\prime}}+\frac{s-1}{2iy^{\prime}}=\frac{s-1}{2iy^{\prime}}\frac{z^{\prime}-\bar{z}}{\bar{z}^{\prime}-\bar{z}},\qquad\qquad\frac{\partial s}{\partial z^{\prime}}=\frac{\bar{z}^{\prime}-\bar{z}}{2yy^{\prime}}-\frac{s-1}{2iy^{\prime}}=\frac{s-1}{2iy^{\prime}}\frac{z-\bar{z}^{\prime}}{z^{\prime}-z},

(65)

4(y^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}s}{\partial z^{\prime}\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}=2s,\qquad\qquad 4(y^{\prime})^{2}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial z^{\prime}}\right)=s^{2}-1.

We now compute:

\displaystyle\Delta_{-t}(\mu^{-}_{g}(z,

\displaystyle z^{\prime}))=\left(-4(y^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z^{\prime}\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}-4ity^{\prime}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)\left(-Q_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(4(y^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{\prime}}+4ity^{\prime}\right)\left(\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}\right)

\displaystyle=4(y^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}s}{\partial z^{\prime}\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}+\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)\left(4(y^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{\prime}}+4ity^{\prime}\right)\left(Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}\right)

\displaystyle=2sQ^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}+4(y^{\prime})^{2}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial z^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle\qquad+4(y^{\prime})^{2}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{\prime}}\left(\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}\right)+4ity^{\prime}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle=2sQ^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}+(s^{2}-1)Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle\qquad+4(y^{\prime})^{2}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(-\frac{2t}{2iy}\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t-1}\right)+4ity^{\prime}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle=2sQ^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}+(s^{2}-1)Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2t}{2iy}\cdot 4(y^{\prime})^{2}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\frac{2iy}{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}+4ity^{\prime}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle=2sQ^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}+(s^{2}-1)Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}

\displaystyle\qquad+4ity^{\prime}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}\left(1+\frac{2iy^{\prime}}{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[(s^{2}-1)Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)+\left(2s+4ity^{\prime}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)\left(1+\frac{2iy^{\prime}}{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}\right)\right)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\right]\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}.

Using (
64
), a quick calculation reveals that

2s+4ity^{\prime}\left(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\bar{z}^{\prime}}\right)\left(1+\frac{2iy^{\prime}}{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}\right)=(2t+2)s-2t.

By plugging this into our previous calculation, we obtain

\Delta_{-t}(\mu^{-}_{g}(z,z^{\prime}))=-\left[(1-s^{2})Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)+(2t-(2t+2)s)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)\right]\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}.

By Corollary
2.3
, we have

(1-s^{2})Q^{\prime\prime}_{k,t}(s)+(2t-(2t+2)s)Q^{\prime}_{k,t}(s)=\lambda_{-t}Q_{k,t}(s).

We deduce that

\Delta_{-t}(\mu^{-}_{g}(z,z^{\prime}))=-\lambda_{-t}Q_{k,t}(s)\left(\frac{\bar{z}-z^{\prime}}{2iy}\right)^{2t}=\lambda_{-t}\mu_{g}^{-}(z,z^{\prime}).

The equality

\Delta_{+t}(\mu^{+}_{g}(z,z^{\prime}))=\lambda_{+t}\mu_{g}^{+}(z,z^{\prime})
can be verified similarly and is left to the reader.
∎

In order to prove property
(iii)
above, it suffices to prove:

Proposition 8.6
.

As
z^{\prime}
approaches the cusp
\infty
, the functions

\Im(\gamma z^{\prime})^{{k\mp t-1}}\langle w^{\pm}(\gamma z^{\prime}),w^{\pm}(\gamma z^{\prime})\rangle\mathcal{G}^{\pm}(z,\gamma z^{\prime})

are
C^{\infty}
for
z^{\prime}
in a neighborhood of
\infty
, for all
\gamma\in\Gamma(M)
.

Proof.

We proceed as in
[
23
, VI §6]
. We retain the notations established in (
62
). As in (
63
), we have

(66)

\Delta_{\pm t}(\mathcal{G}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime}))=\lambda_{\pm t}\mathcal{G}^{\pm}(z,z^{\prime}).

Begin by observing using (
57
) that for all
\gamma\in\Gamma(M)
, we have

\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,\gamma z^{\prime})=j(\gamma,z^{\prime})^{-2t}\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime}).

In particular, letting
A_{M}:=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&M\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Gamma(M)
, we see that

\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime}+M)=\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,A_{M}z^{\prime})=j(A_{M},z^{\prime})^{-2t}\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime})=\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime}).

Thus, when viewed as a function in the single variable
z^{\prime}=x^{\prime}+iy^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{G^{-}}
is
M
-periodic and admits a Fourier expansion. More precisely, for
z\in E\subset\mathcal{H}
with
E
compact and
y^{\prime}\geq M_{E}+1
with
M_{E}>0
as in
[
23
, Prop. 6.6]
, we have

\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}c^{-}_{n}(z,y^{\prime})e^{2\pi i\frac{n}{M}x^{\prime}}.

As a function of
y^{\prime}
, the
n^{\text{th}}
Fourier coefficient

(67)

c_{n}^{-}(z,y^{\prime})=\frac{1}{M}\int_{0}^{M}\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,x^{\prime}+iy^{\prime})e^{-2\pi i\frac{n}{M}x^{\prime}}dx^{\prime}

satisfies the differential equation

(68)

u^{\prime\prime}(y^{\prime})-\frac{2t}{y^{\prime}}u^{\prime}(y^{\prime})-\bigg[\left(\frac{2\pi n}{M}\right)^{2}+\frac{4\pi nt}{y^{\prime}M}+\frac{(k-t-1)(k+t)}{(y^{\prime})^{2}}\bigg]u(y^{\prime})=0.

This is readily verified by differentiating (
67
) under the sign of the integral, using the equality of differential operators

\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial(y^{\prime})^{2}}-\frac{2t}{y^{\prime}}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{\prime}}=-\frac{1}{(y^{\prime})^{2}}\Delta_{-t}-\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial(x^{\prime})^{2}}+\frac{2it}{y^{\prime}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\right)

coupled with the equality (
66
),
and by integrating by parts. We identify the ordinary differential equation (
68
) as being a confluent differential equation. In the notations of
[
16
, 6.2 (13)]
, (
68
) corresponds to the case

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=-2t

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{2\pi n}{M}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\pi nt}{M}

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\lambda_{-t}=-(k-t-1)(k+t).

For
n\neq 0
, we note that
a^{2}\neq 4\alpha
, and thus solutions of (
68
) are given by
[
16
, 6.2 (14)]

(y^{\prime})^{t}w\left(-t,k-\frac{1}{2};\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right),

where
w(\kappa,\mu;x)
is any solution of the Whittaker differential equation
[
16
, 6.1 (4)]

(69)

z^{\prime\prime}(x)+\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\kappa}{x}+\frac{1/4-\mu^{2}}{x^{2}}\right)z(x)=0.

Note that when
t=0
, (
68
) reduces to the differential equation

(70)

u^{\prime\prime}(y^{\prime})-\bigg[\left(\frac{2\pi n}{M}\right)^{2}+\frac{k(k-1)}{(y^{\prime})^{2}}\bigg]u(y^{\prime})=0.

This is compatible with
[
23
, Prop. 4.10 (ii)]
, in which solutions are given by modified Bessel functions. Indeed, the Whittaker differential equation (
69
) collapses in the case
\kappa=-t=0
to

z^{\prime\prime}(x)+\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1/4-\mu^{2}}{x^{2}}\right)z(x)=0,

which can be transformed into a modified Bessel differential equation.

Upon noting that
2\mu+1=2k\in\mathbb{Z}
, two linearly independent solutions of equation (
69
) are given by the Whittaker functions

M_{\kappa,\mu}(x):=e^{-x/2}x^{\mu+1/2}\Phi(\mu-\kappa+1/2,2\mu+1;x),

W_{\kappa,\mu}(x):=e^{-x/2}x^{\mu+1/2}\Psi(\mu-\kappa+1/2,2\mu+1;x).

The functions
\Phi
and
\Psi
are the confluent hypergeometric functions of the first and second kind, respectively:
\Phi
is Kummer’s function defined by

\Phi(a,c;x):=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{(a)_{n}}{(c_{n})}\frac{x^{n}}{n!},

and
\Psi
is Tricomi’s function defined by the integral formula
[
16
, 6.5 (3)]
.

We deduce that for
n\neq 0
, there exist functions
a_{n}
and
b_{n}
such that

c_{n}^{-}(z,y^{\prime})=a_{n}(z)(y^{\prime})^{t}M_{-t,k-1/2}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right)+b_{n}(z)(y^{\prime})^{t}W_{-t,k-1/2}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right).

Explicitly, we have

M_{-t,k-1/2}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right)=e^{-\frac{2\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right)^{k}\Phi\left(k+t,2k;\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right),

W_{-t,k-1/2}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right)=e^{-\frac{2\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right)^{k}\Psi\left(k+t,2k;\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right).

When
n=0
,

c_{0}^{-}(z,y^{\prime})
satisfies the differential equation

(71)

u^{\prime\prime}(y^{\prime})-\frac{2t}{y^{\prime}}u^{\prime}(y^{\prime})-\frac{(k-t-1)(k+t)}{(y^{\prime})^{2}}u(y^{\prime})=0.

Thus, there exist functions
a_{0}
and
b_{0}
such that

c_{0}^{-}(z,y^{\prime})=a_{0}(z)(y^{\prime})^{k+t}+b_{0}(z)(y^{\prime})^{t-k+1}.

By the same arguments as in
[
23
, Prop. 4.12]
, we see that
a_{n}(z)=0
for all
n\neq 0
because the confluent hypergeometric function of the first kind
\Phi
becomes exponentially large at
y^{\prime}=\infty
, as is clear from the integral representation
[
16
, 6.5 (1)]
. (Note that the integration condition in
[
23
, Prop. 4.12]
holds in our case by (
60
).)
Therefore, as in
[
23
, (6.5)]
, we see that

\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime})=a_{0}(z)(y^{\prime})^{k+t}+b_{0}(z)(y^{\prime})^{t-k+1}+\sum_{n\neq 0}b_{n}(z)(y^{\prime})^{t}W_{-t,k-1/2}\left(\frac{4\pi n}{M}y^{\prime}\right)e^{2\pi i\frac{n}{M}x^{\prime}}.

It follows that
(y^{\prime})^{{k-t-1}}\mathcal{G}^{-}(z,z^{\prime})
is
C^{\infty}
for
z^{\prime}
in a neighborhood of the cusp
\infty
, and the result follows for
\gamma=I_{2}
upon noting that
(y^{\prime})^{{k+t-1}}\langle w^{-}(z^{\prime}),w^{-}(z^{\prime})\rangle=(y^{\prime})^{{k-t-1}}
. The case of the other cusps can be deduced similarly, and is left to the reader. The proof for
\mathcal{G}^{+}
is obtained by replacing all occurrences of
-t
in the above proof by
+t
.
∎

9.
Brylinski pairings

We continue to use the notation
C=Y(M)_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Having proved that the section
G=G_{k,t,M}
of Lemma
8.2
is the Green’s kernel associated to the vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
(
33
) on
C
, we are now ready to compute archimedean Brylinski pairings (
23
) between what Brylinski calls “Hodge classes” of
\mathcal{W}
. These are sums of classes of type
(0,0)
in the fibers
W_{z}
, for
z\in C
.

We will be interested in cycles
Z_{1},Z_{2}\in\CH^{k+t}(X_{\mathbb{C}})_{K}
which are homologically trivial and supported in fibers above
z_{1},z_{2}\in C
. Their local height pairing depends only on the image of these classes in
W_{z_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}
by (
24
). We therefore work with
\mathbb{C}
coefficients, and compute the pairing for the basic sections
w^{\pm}
.

9.1.
Brylinski’s pairing on
C

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
z_{1}=x_{1}+iy_{1}\in\mathcal{H}
and
z_{2}=x_{2}+iy_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
with
z_{1}\not\in\Gamma(M)z_{2}
. Then the Brylinski pairing is given by

\langle w^{-}(z_{1}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{1}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t},

\langle w^{+}(z_{1}),w^{+}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{1}{(2i)^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(z_{1}-\gamma\bar{z}_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,\bar{z}_{2})^{2t},

where

g(z,z^{\prime})=-Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2yy^{\prime}}\right).

Moreover, we have

\langle w^{-}(z_{1}),w^{+}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\langle w^{+}(z_{1}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=0.

Proof.

The Green’s kernel associated to the bundle
\mathcal{W}
on
C
is given by
G=G_{k,t,M}
of Lemma
8.2
. Let
Q_{i}=\pr(z_{i})=\Gamma(M)z_{i}\in C(\mathbb{C})
for
i=1,2
. By assumption, we have
Q_{1}\neq Q_{2}
. By definition of Brylinski’s pairing (
23
) in this case, if
w_{1}\in\mathcal{W}_{Q_{1}}
and
w_{2}\in\mathcal{W}_{Q_{2}}
, then

\langle w_{1},w_{2}\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}:=\langle G(Q_{1},Q_{2})(w_{1}),w_{2}\rangle_{Q_{2}},

where
\langle\>,\>\rangle_{Q_{2}}
is the Hermitian pairing (associated to the polarization) of
\mathcal{W}
restricted to the fiber at
Q_{2}
.

We then compute

\displaystyle\langle w^{-}(z_{1}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\langle G(Q_{1},Q_{2})(w^{-}(z_{1})),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle_{Q_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t}\mu^{-}_{g}(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})w^{-}(z_{2}),w^{-}(z_{2})\right\rangle_{Q_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})\left(\frac{\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2}}{2iy_{1}}\right)^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t}\langle w^{-}(z_{2}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle_{Q_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2iy_{1})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t}\langle w^{-}(z_{2}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle_{z_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t},

where in the fifth equality we used the formula
\langle w^{-}(z_{2}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle_{z_{2}}=y_{2}^{-2t}
.
The calculation for
w^{+}
can be done similarly, using the equality
\langle w^{+}(z_{2}),w^{+}(z_{2})\rangle_{z_{2}}=y_{2}^{2t}
. The equalities

\langle w^{-}(z_{1}),w^{+}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\langle w^{+}(z_{1}),w^{-}(z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=0

are deduced in a similar way from the fact that
w_{k-t-1,2t}(z)
and
w_{k+t-1,0}(z)
are orthogonal with respect to the Hermitian pairing
\langle\>,\>\rangle_{z}
.
∎

9.2.
Working over
X_{0}(N)

So far we have worked with varieties fibered over
\bar{C}=X(M)
, as well as vector bundles over
\bar{C}
. Ultimately, we wish to compute the heights of generalized Heegner cycles “over
X_{0}(N)
”.

Denote by
\pi_{M,N}\colon X(M){\longrightarrow\,}X_{0}(N)
the natural quotient map with Galois group

S=S_{M,N}:=\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N).

Recall from (
27
) and (
28
) that we defined generalised Heegner cycles above the Heegner point
P_{\mathfrak{n}}=(A,A[\mathfrak{n}])\in X_{0}(N)(H)
as the sum of the generalized Heegner cycles over the pre-image CM-points
Q\in X(M)(\mathbb{C})
of
P_{\mathfrak{n}}
with respect to
\pi_{M,N}
(similar to what is done in
[
41
, §4.1]
).

Given
P=\Gamma_{0}(N)z\in X_{0}(N)(\mathbb{C})
, define

(72)

w^{\pm}(P):=\sum_{s\in S}s\cdot w^{\pm}(z)\in\sum_{Q\in\pi_{M,N}^{-1}(P)}\mathcal{W}_{Q}=:\mathcal{W}_{P},

the action being the one defined in (
51
).

Remark 9.2
.

We say a few words about the action of
S
on elements
w^{\pm}(\Gamma(M)z)
in fibers of the bundle
\mathcal{W}
. Let
[\gamma]=s\in S=\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)
with
\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)
. Then
s\cdot\Gamma(M)z:=\Gamma(M)\gamma z
. Consider now the variety
\pi\colon X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}
fibered over
\bar{C}
. The fiber
\pi^{-1}(\Gamma(M)z)=(\mathbb{C}/\langle 1,z\rangle)^{2k-2}\times A^{2t}
consists of points
x=(\Gamma(M)z,w_{1},\ldots,w_{2k-2},P_{1},\ldots,P_{2t})
with
w_{i}\in\mathbb{C}/\langle 1,z\rangle
and
P_{j}\in A
. The action of
[\gamma]=s\in S
is given by

s\cdot x:=(\Gamma(M)\gamma z,w_{1},\ldots,w_{2k-2},P_{1},\ldots,P_{2t})\in\pi^{-1}(\Gamma(M)\gamma z).

Fixing a point
\Gamma_{0}(N)z\in X_{0}(N)(\mathbb{C})
,
S
acts simply transitively on the fibers
\pi^{-1}(\Gamma(M)z_{i})
with
\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{i}=\Gamma_{0}(N)z
. This action on
X
gives rise to a correspondence on
X
, hence an action on Chow groups and cohomology groups. There is also an induced action on the bundle
\mathcal{W}
, which we now address. Recall from the beginning of §
8.2
that the fibers
\tilde{\mathcal{W}_{z}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\alpha z}
are identified via the action (
51
) of
\gamma
on fibers for all
\alpha\in\Gamma(M)
. In other words, we have identifications of fibers

(73)

\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma(M)z}=\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}\overset{\alpha\cdot}{\simeq}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\alpha z},\qquad\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Gamma(M).

Now, the action of
[\gamma]=s\in S=\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)
on a section
w
of
\mathcal{W}
is given by

s\cdot w(\Gamma(M)z)=\gamma\cdot w(z).

The latter action is via (
51
) and we must interpret
\gamma\cdot w(z)
as lying in
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\gamma z}\simeq\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma(M)\gamma z}=\mathcal{W}_{s\cdot\Gamma(M)z}
. This is well-defined because if
\alpha\in\Gamma(M)
, then

(\alpha\gamma)\cdot w(z)=\alpha\cdot(\gamma\cdot w(z))\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\alpha\gamma z},

and under the identification (
73
) the latter element corresponds to
\gamma\cdot w(z)\in\mathcal{W}_{s\cdot\Gamma(M)z}
.

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
z_{1}=x_{1}+iy_{1}\in\mathcal{H}
and
z_{2}=x_{2}+iy_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
with
P_{1}:=\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{1}\neq\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{2}=:P_{2}
. Then the Brylinski pairing is given by

\langle w^{-}(P_{1}),w^{-}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{|S|}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\penalty\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t},

\langle w^{+}(P_{1}),w^{+}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{|S|}{(2i)^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\penalty\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(z_{1}-\gamma\bar{z}_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,\bar{z}_{2})^{2t},

where

g(z,z^{\prime})=-Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2yy^{\prime}}\right).

Moreover, we have

\langle w^{-}(P_{1}),w^{+}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\langle w^{+}(P_{1}),w^{-}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=0.

Proof.

By definition of Brylinski’s pairing and (
72
), we have

\displaystyle\langle w^{\pm}(P_{1}),w^{\pm}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\sum_{s\in S}s\cdot w^{\pm}(z_{1}),\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S}s^{\prime}\cdot w^{\pm}(z_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S}\left\langle s\cdot w^{\pm}(z_{1}),\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S}ss^{-1}s^{\prime}\cdot w^{\pm}(z_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S}\left\langle w^{\pm}(z_{1}),\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S}s^{-1}s^{\prime}\cdot w^{\pm}(z_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=|S|\sum_{s\in S}\left\langle w^{\pm}(z_{1}),s\cdot w^{\pm}(z_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}},

where we used the invariance
\langle\sigma\cdot w,\sigma\cdot w^{\prime}\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}
for any automorphism
\sigma
of
\bar{C}
by functoriality.
If we choose a lift
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)
of
s\in S=\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)
, then
\Gamma(M)sz_{2}=\Gamma(M)\gamma^{\prime}z_{2}
.
Moreover, we have
\Gamma(M)z_{1}\neq\Gamma(M)\gamma^{\prime}z_{2}
since otherwise
\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{1}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\gamma^{\prime}z_{2}=\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{2}
, which contradicts our assumptions.
We may thus apply Theorem
9.1
and use (
52
) along with the fact that the Brylinski pairing is Hermitian to obtain

\displaystyle\langle w^{-}(P_{1}),w^{-}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=|S|\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)}\left\langle w^{-}(z_{1}),\gamma^{\prime}\cdot w^{-}(z_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=|S|\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)}\frac{j(\gamma^{\prime},z_{2})^{2t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma\gamma^{\prime}z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma\gamma^{\prime}z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}z_{2})^{2t}.

By noting that
j(\gamma^{\prime},z_{2})j(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}z_{2})=j(\gamma\gamma^{\prime},z_{2})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\langle w^{-}(P_{1}),w^{-}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\pm\Gamma(M)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(z_{1},\gamma\gamma^{\prime}z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma\gamma^{\prime}z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma\gamma^{\prime},z_{2})^{2t}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\penalty\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t}.

The proof for
w^{+}
is similar.
∎

9.3.
Action of Hecke operators

As the vector bundles
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
and
\mathcal{W}
are equipped with a
{\rm GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
-action, the Hecke operators
T_{m}
act on sections such as
w^{+}
and
w^{-}
.
Let

R_{N}:=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbb{Z}&\mathbb{Z}\\
N\mathbb{Z}&\mathbb{Z}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

and consider, for
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

R_{N}^{m}:=\Gamma_{0}(N)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&m\end{smallmatrix}\right)\Gamma_{0}(N)=\{\gamma\in R_{N}\mid\det(\gamma)=m\}\subset{\rm GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}.

The double coset
R_{N}^{m}
naturally carries a left action of
\Gamma_{0}(N)
by left multiplication.

Let
z\in\mathcal{H}
with
P:=\Gamma_{0}(N)z
. Given
m\geq 1
, we set

(74)

T_{m}w^{\pm}(P):=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}\gamma\cdot w^{\pm}(P),

where the action is analogous to the one defined in (
51
), i.e.,

\gamma\cdot w^{\pm}(P):=\det(\gamma)^{p\mp t}j(\gamma,z)^{\pm 2t}w^{\pm}(\Gamma_{0}(N)\gamma z),

where a representative of
\gamma
in
R^{m}_{N}
has implicitly been chosen.

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
with
P_{i}=\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{i},

i=1,2
, and let
m\geq 1
with
(m,N)=1
. Assume that
P_{1}
and
T_{m}P_{2}
are disjoint in
X_{0}(N)
. Then

\langle w^{-}(P_{1}),T_{m}w^{-}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{|S|m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in R^{m}_{N}/\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t},

\langle w^{+}(P_{1}),T_{m}w^{+}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{|S|m^{p-t}}{(2i)^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in R^{m}_{N}/\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(z_{1}-\gamma\bar{z}_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,\bar{z}_{2})^{2t}.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\langle

\displaystyle w^{-}(P_{1}),T_{m}w^{-}(P_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{z}_{2})^{-2t}\langle w^{-}(\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{1}),w^{-}(\Gamma_{0}(N)\gamma z_{2})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{z}_{2})^{-2t}\frac{|S|}{(2iy_{1}\Im(\gamma z_{2}))^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\penalty\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma^{\prime}\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma z_{2})^{2t}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\penalty\pm 1}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R^{m}_{N}}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t}g(z_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma^{\prime}\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma z_{2})^{2t}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\penalty\pm 1}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}g(z_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma^{\prime}\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,z_{2})^{2t}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in R^{m}_{N}/\penalty\pm 1}g(z_{1},\gamma z_{2})(\bar{z}_{1}-\gamma z_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,z_{2})^{2t},

where we used Theorem
9.3
in the second equality and the identity
j(\gamma,z)j(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma z)=j(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,z)
in the fourth equality. The calculation for
w^{+}
is similar.
∎

9.4.
Brylinski’s pairing over Heegner points on
X_{0}(N)

We now specialize to the case where
P_{1}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{1}
and
P_{2}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{2}
are Heegner points on
X_{0}(N)
of conductor
1
. In terms of moduli, this means that these points represent isomorphism classes of cyclic
N
-isogenies between elliptic curves with CM by the same order in an imaginary quadratic field (in our case the imaginary quadratic field is
K
and the order is the maximal order
\mathcal{O}_{K}
). Concretely, following
[
22
, §2]
, Heegner points with CM by
K
are indexed by
(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n})
, where
\mathfrak{n}
is a cyclic
N
-ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
(whose existence is guaranteed by the Heegner hypothesis) and
\mathcal{A}\in\mathrm{Cl}_{K}
. Over
\mathbb{C}
, the corresponding point is
(\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1})
for any
[\mathfrak{a}]=\mathcal{A}
. If
W_{\mathrm{AL}}
denotes the group of Atkin–Lehner involutions of
X_{0}(N)
, then
{\rm Gal}(H/K)\times W_{\mathrm{AL}}
acts simply transitively on the set of all Heegner points of discriminant
D
, i.e., on all pairs
(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n})
. If
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
corresponds to
\mathcal{B}\in\Cl_{K}
under the Artin map of class field theory, then
\sigma(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n})=(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}^{-1},\mathfrak{n})
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{Z}/2N\mathbb{Z}
with
\beta^{2}\equiv D\pmod{4N}
such that
\mathfrak{n}=\langle N,\frac{\beta+\sqrt{D}}{2}\rangle
. Let
\tau=\frac{-B+\sqrt{D}}{2A}\in\mathcal{H}
7
7

7

Beware of the following conflict of notation:
A
denotes our fixed CM elliptic curve,
B
a full level
M
-structure, and
C
the modular curve
Y(M)
. We trust that this slight abuse of notation will not cause any confusion.
be the solution to a quadratic equation of the form

AX^{2}+BX+C=0,\quad A>0,\quad B^{2}-4AC=D,\quad N\mid A,\quad B\equiv\beta\pmod{2N}

and consider

\mathfrak{a}=\left\langle A,\frac{B+\sqrt{D}}{2}\right\rangle,\qquad\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1}=\left\langle\frac{A}{N},\frac{B+\sqrt{D}}{2}\right\rangle,\qquad N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})=A,\qquad[\mathfrak{a}]=\mathcal{A}.

Then the Heegner point associated to
(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n})
is represented by
-\bar{\tau}=(B+\sqrt{D})/2A\in\mathcal{H}
.
Note that

\mathfrak{a}^{-1}=\frac{\bar{\mathfrak{a}}}{N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}=\left\langle 1,\frac{-B+\sqrt{D}}{2A}\right\rangle=\langle 1,\tau\rangle,

whence the Heegner point associated to
(\mathcal{A}^{-1},\bar{\mathfrak{n}})
is represented by
\tau\in\mathcal{H}
.
8
8

8

See the Appendix for a discussion of how our conventions for Heegner points differ slightly from the ones in
[
22
]
.

Now, for
i=1,2
,
P_{i}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{i}
is a Heegner point, hence
\langle 1,\tau_{i}\rangle=\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{-1}
for some ideal
\mathfrak{a}_{i}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Let
A_{i}>0
and
B_{i}
be the corresponding integers as above such that
\tau_{i}=\frac{-B_{i}+\sqrt{D}}{2A_{i}}=\tau_{\mathcal{A}_{i},\mathfrak{n}_{i}}
and
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a}_{i})=A_{i}
.

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
P_{1}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{1}
and
P_{2}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{2}
be two Heegner points of
X_{0}(N)
and suppose that
P_{1}
and
T_{m}P_{2}
are disjoint in
X_{0}(N)
. With notations as above, we have

\left\langle\frac{w^{-}(P_{1})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})},T_{m}\frac{w^{-}(P_{2})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{2})}\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{(-1)^{t}|S|2^{2t}m^{p-t}}{|D|^{2t}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t},

\left\langle\frac{w^{+}(P_{1})}{y_{1}^{2t}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1})},T_{m}\frac{w^{+}(P_{2})}{y_{2}^{2t}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{(-1)^{t}|S|2^{2t}m^{p-t}}{|D|^{2t}}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})\sum_{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\bar{\tau}_{2})^{2t},

where
\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=c\bar{\tau}_{1}\tau_{2}+d\bar{\tau}_{1}-a\tau_{2}-b
for
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Proof.

Observe that
\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{i}\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{i})=\chi(N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a}_{i}))=\chi(A_{i})=A_{i}^{2t}
. In particular,
\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{i})^{-1}=\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{i})/A_{i}^{2t}
. Using the fact that the Brylinski pairing is skew-symmetric, we obtain

\left\langle\frac{w^{-}(P_{1})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})},T_{m}\frac{w^{-}(P_{2})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{2})}\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})}{(A_{1}A_{2})^{2t}}\left\langle w^{-}(P_{1}),T_{m}w^{-}(P_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}.

Observe that
y_{i}=\sqrt{|D|}/2A_{i}
and apply Proposition
9.4
to obtain

\left\langle\frac{w^{-}(P_{1})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})},T_{m}\frac{w^{-}(P_{2})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{2})}\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=\frac{(-1)^{t}|S|2^{2t}m^{p-t}}{|D|^{2t}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\gamma\in R^{m}_{N}/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})(\bar{\tau}_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2})^{2t}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})^{2t}.

As
(\bar{\tau}_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=(\bar{\tau}_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2})(c\tau_{2}+d)=c\bar{\tau_{1}}\tau_{2}+d\bar{\tau}_{1}-a\tau_{2}-b=\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
, the first equality follows.
The calculation for
w^{+}
is similar.
∎

10.
Cycle classes

The goal of this section is to compute the cycle classes of generalized Heegner cycles viewed as cycles in CM fibers of
X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}
. While the cycles are null-homologous in
X
by Lemma
5.1
, this is not necessarily true when viewed as cycles in the fibers.

10.1.
Preliminary cycle class calculations

For an elliptic curve
E/\mathbb{C}
, we will view
\End(E)
as a subring of
\mathbb{C}
via
\End(E)\hookrightarrow\End(H^{0}(E,\Omega_{E}))\simeq\mathbb{C}
. In this subsection and the next, we view
E
as a complex manifold and work exclusively with de Rham cohomology of complex manifolds.

Proposition 10.1
.

Suppose
E=\mathbb{C}/\langle 1,z\rangle
for some
z=x+iy\in\mathcal{H}
with complex coordinate
w
. For endomorphisms
\alpha,\beta\in\End(E)
, define
\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta}=(\alpha\times\beta)_{*}([E])\in\CH^{1}(E\times E)

and

X_{\alpha,\beta}:=\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta}-\deg(\beta)\Gamma_{0,1}-\deg(\alpha)\Gamma_{1,0}\in\CH^{1}(E\times E).

Then

\cl(X_{\alpha,\beta})=\frac{i}{2y}(\alpha\bar{\beta}d\bar{w}_{1}\otimes dw_{2}-\bar{\alpha}\beta dw_{1}\otimes d\bar{w}_{2})\in H_{\dR}^{1}(E)\otimes H_{\dR}^{1}(E)

is the orthogonal projection of
\cl(\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta})
to
H_{\dR}^{1}(E)\otimes H_{\dR}^{1}(E)
.

Proof.

If
Z\subset E\times E
is a divisor, then its de Rham cycle class
\omega_{Z}\in H^{2}_{\dR}(E\times E)
is characterized by the property

\int_{Z}\eta=\int_{E\times E}\eta\wedge\omega_{Z},\qquad\text{ for all }\eta\in H^{2}_{\dR}(E\times E).

By Künneth, a basis of
H^{2}_{\dR}(E\times E)
is given by

\begin{array}[]{ll}b_{1\bar{1}}:=\pr_{1}^{*}(dw\wedge d\bar{w}),&b_{12}:=\pr_{1}^{*}(dw)\wedge\pr_{2}^{*}(dw),\\
b_{1\bar{2}}:=\pr_{1}^{*}(dw)\wedge\pr_{2}^{*}(d\bar{w}),&b_{\bar{1}2}:=\pr_{1}^{*}(d\bar{w})\wedge\pr_{2}^{*}(dw),\\
b_{\bar{1}\bar{2}}:=\pr_{1}^{*}(d\bar{w})\wedge\pr_{2}^{*}(d\bar{w}),&b_{2\bar{2}}:=\pr_{2}^{*}(dw\wedge d\bar{w}).\end{array}

Let

\eta=\lambda_{1\bar{1}}b_{1\bar{1}}+\lambda_{12}b_{12}+\lambda_{1\bar{2}}b_{1\bar{2}}+\lambda_{\bar{1}2}b_{\bar{1}2}+\lambda_{\bar{1}\bar{2}}b_{\bar{1}\bar{2}}+\lambda_{2\bar{2}}b_{2\bar{2}}\in H^{2}_{\dR}(E\times E).

Begin by observing that

(75)

\cl(0\times E)=\frac{i}{2y}b_{1\bar{1}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\cl(E\times 0)=\frac{i}{2y}b_{2\bar{2}}.

Indeed, on one hand we have

\int_{0\times E}\eta=\int_{0\times E}\lambda_{2\bar{2}}b_{2\bar{2}}=\lambda_{2\bar{2}}\int_{E}dw\wedge d\bar{w}=-2iy\lambda_{2\bar{2}},

while on the other hand

\int_{E\times E}\eta\wedge\frac{i}{2y}b_{1\bar{1}}=\frac{\lambda_{2\bar{2}}i}{2y}\int_{E\times E}b_{1\bar{1}}b_{2\bar{2}}=\frac{\lambda_{2\bar{2}}i}{2y}\left(\int_{E}dw\wedge d\bar{w}\right)^{2}=\frac{-\lambda_{2\bar{2}}i4y^{2}}{2y}=-2iy\lambda_{2\bar{2}}.

The case of
E\times 0
is verified similarly.

On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta}}\eta

\displaystyle=\int_{E}\lambda_{1\bar{1}}\alpha^{*}dw\wedge\alpha^{*}d\bar{w}+\lambda_{1\bar{2}}\alpha^{*}dw\wedge\beta^{*}d\bar{w}+\lambda_{\bar{1}2}\alpha^{*}d\bar{w}\wedge\beta^{*}dw+\lambda_{2\bar{2}}\beta^{*}dw\wedge\beta^{*}d\bar{w}

\displaystyle=-2iy(\lambda_{1\bar{1}}\alpha\bar{\alpha}+\lambda_{1\bar{2}}\alpha\bar{\beta}-\lambda_{\bar{1}2}\bar{\alpha}\beta+\lambda_{2\bar{2}}\beta\bar{\beta}).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{E\times E}\eta\wedge

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2y}(\beta\bar{\beta}b_{1\bar{1}}+\alpha\bar{\beta}b_{\bar{1}2}-\bar{\alpha}\beta b_{1\bar{2}}+\alpha\bar{\alpha}b_{2\bar{2}})

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2y}\int_{E\times E}(\lambda_{2\bar{2}}\beta\bar{\beta}+\lambda_{1\bar{2}}\alpha\bar{\beta}-\lambda_{\bar{1}2}\bar{\alpha}\beta+\lambda_{1\bar{1}}\alpha\bar{\alpha})b_{1\bar{1}}\wedge b_{2\bar{2}}

\displaystyle=-2iy(\lambda_{2\bar{2}}\beta\bar{\beta}+\lambda_{1\bar{2}}\alpha\bar{\beta}-\lambda_{\bar{1}2}\bar{\alpha}\beta+\lambda_{1\bar{1}}\alpha\bar{\alpha}).

We conclude that

\cl(\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta})=\frac{i}{2y}(\beta\bar{\beta}b_{1\bar{1}}+\alpha\bar{\beta}b_{\bar{1}2}-\bar{\alpha}\beta b_{1\bar{2}}+\alpha\bar{\alpha}b_{2\bar{2}}).

Using the fact that
\alpha\bar{\alpha}=\deg(\alpha)
and
\beta\bar{\beta}=\deg(\beta)
combined with (
75
), we obtain

\cl(\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta})=\deg(\beta)\cl(0\times E)+\deg(\alpha)\cl(E\times 0)+\frac{i}{2y}(\alpha\bar{\beta}b_{\bar{1}2}-\bar{\alpha}\beta b_{1\bar{2}}).

∎

10.2.
Cycle classes of generalized Heegner cycles

For the rest of the section, we view the varieties
C
,
X
,
A
, etc. as being complex manifolds.

Definition 10.2
.

Given
\Gamma(M)z\in C(\mathbb{C})
, let

\cl_{z}\colon\CH^{k+t-1}(E_{z}^{2k-2}\times A^{2t}){\longrightarrow\,}\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}=\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma(M)z}

denote the
\epsilon_{W}\otimes\kappa_{2t}
-component of the cycle class map on the fiber
E_{z}^{2k-2}\times A^{2t}
of
\pi\colon X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}
above
\Gamma(M)z
. If
P\in X_{0}(N)(\mathbb{C})
, let

\cl_{P}:=\sum_{\Gamma(M)z\in\pi_{M,N}^{-1}(P)}\cl_{z}\colon\sum_{\Gamma(M)z\in\pi_{M,N}^{-1}(P)}\CH^{k+t-1}(E_{z}^{2k-2}\times A^{2t}){\longrightarrow\,}\sum_{\Gamma(M)z\in\pi_{M,N}^{-1}(P)}\mathcal{W}_{\Gamma(M)z}=:\mathcal{W}_{P}.

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}
an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and let
\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}=x_{\mathfrak{a}}+iy_{\mathfrak{a}}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
A^{\mathfrak{a}}=\mathbb{C}/\langle 1,\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}\rangle
.
Then

\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})=\frac{(-1)^{p}2^{p-t}\sqrt{|D|}^{p-t}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}^{1/2}}y_{0}^{t}w^{-}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}

and

\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\bar{\epsilon}Y^{\mathfrak{a}})=\frac{(-1)^{p}2^{p-t}\sqrt{|D|}^{p-t}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}^{1/2}}\frac{y_{0}^{t}}{y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2t}}w^{+}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})\in\tilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}.

Proof.

We recall that

Y^{\mathfrak{a}}=(\Gamma_{[\sqrt{D}]})^{p-t}\times(\Gamma_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}^{T})^{2t}\subset(A^{\mathfrak{a}}\times A^{\mathfrak{a}})^{p-t}\times(A^{\mathfrak{a}}\times A)^{2t}.

Replacing
\Gamma_{[\sqrt{D}]}
by
X_{1,[\sqrt{D}]}
in this definition has no effect on
\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}
and
\bar{\epsilon}Y^{\mathfrak{a}}
(the effect of Scholl’s projector
\epsilon_{W}
is precisely to project the cycle classes (in fibers) to
\sym^{2p}H_{\dR}^{1}(A^{\mathfrak{a}})\subset H^{1}_{\dR}(A^{\mathfrak{a}})^{\otimes 2p}
). By Proposition
10.1
applied with
(\alpha,\beta)=(1,[\sqrt{D}])
, we have

\cl(X_{1,[\sqrt{D}]})=-\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2y_{\mathfrak{a}}}(dw_{1}\otimes d\bar{w}_{2}+d\bar{w}_{1}\otimes dw_{2})\in H^{1}_{\dR}(A^{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes H^{1}_{\dR}(A^{\mathfrak{a}}).

Projecting to the symmetric power, it follows that

\cl(\epsilon_{1}X_{1,\sqrt{D}})=-\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{y_{\mathfrak{a}}}dw_{1}d\bar{w}_{2}\in\sym^{2}H^{1}_{\dR}(A^{\mathfrak{a}}).

Observe that
\Gamma_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}^{T}=\Gamma_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a},1}}=\{(\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}(x),x)\mid x\in A\}\subset A^{\mathfrak{a}}\times A
. After applying the relevant projectors, only the orthogonal projection of the cycle class to
H^{1}_{\dR}(A^{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes H_{\dR}^{1}(A)
will contribute. We therefore calculate the cycle class of

X_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}},1}:=\Gamma_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}},1}-0\times A-\frac{y_{0}}{y_{\mathfrak{a}}}\cdot A^{\mathfrak{a}}\times 0\in\CH^{1}(A^{\mathfrak{a}}\times A)\;

by the same method as in the proof of Proposition
10.1
to obtain

\cl(X_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}},1})=\frac{i}{2y_{\mathfrak{a}}}(d\bar{w}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes dw-dw_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes d\bar{w})\in H_{\dR}^{1}(A^{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes H_{\dR}^{1}(A),

where
w_{\mathfrak{a}}
is the complex coordinate on
A^{\mathfrak{a}}
and
w
is the one on
A
.
The effect of the projector
\epsilon
is to kill
d\bar{w}
. Hence, we obtain

\cl(\epsilon X_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}},1})=\frac{i}{2y_{\mathfrak{a}}}d\bar{w}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes dw\quad\text{ and }\quad\cl(\bar{\epsilon}X_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}},1})=-\frac{i}{2y_{\mathfrak{a}}}dw_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes d\bar{w}.

Recall from Section
6.4
that
dw_{\mathfrak{a}}
corresponds to
\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}u_{1}+u_{2}
and
dw
corresponds to
\tau_{0}e_{1}+e_{2}
.
Putting everything together, we conclude that

\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})=\left(-\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{y_{\mathfrak{a}}}\right)^{p-t}\left(\frac{i}{2y_{\mathfrak{a}}}\right)^{2t}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}(\bar{\tau}_{\mathfrak{a}}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}\otimes(\tau_{0}e_{1}+e_{0})^{2t}

and
