\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\bar{\epsilon}Y^{\mathfrak{a}})=\left(-\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{y_{\mathfrak{a}}}\right)^{p-t}\left(-\frac{i}{2y_{\mathfrak{a}}}\right)^{2t}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p+t}(\bar{\tau}_{\mathfrak{a}}u_{1}+u_{2})^{p-t}\otimes(\bar{\tau}_{0}e_{1}+e_{0})^{2t}.

Now compare with the definitions of
w^{-}
and
w^{+}
.
∎

Given an ideal class
\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}
represented by an integral ideal
\mathfrak{a}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
coprime to
M
, we recall from (
27
) and (
28
) the cycles

Z_{\mathcal{A}}:=\chi(\mathfrak{a})^{-1}\sum_{Q\in\pi_{M,N}^{-1}(P_{\mathfrak{n}})}\epsilon Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}}\in\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0,K(\chi)},

\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}:=\bar{\chi}(\mathfrak{a})^{-1}\sum_{Q\in\pi_{M,N}^{-1}(P_{\mathfrak{n}})}\bar{\epsilon}Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}}\in\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0,K(\chi)}.

Recall also the notation
Z=Z_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}]}
and
\bar{Z}=\bar{Z}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}]}
.

Consider the Heegner points
P_{\mathfrak{n}}:=(A{\longrightarrow\,}A/A[\mathfrak{n}])
and
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}:=(A^{\mathfrak{a}}{\longrightarrow\,}A^{\mathfrak{a}}/A^{\mathfrak{a}}[\mathfrak{n}])
in
X_{0}(N)(H)
. If
[\mathfrak{a}]=\mathcal{A}
, then the generalized Heegner cycles
Z_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}
respectively lie in the fibers above the latter point with respect to
\pi_{M,N}\circ\pi\colon X{\longrightarrow\,}X(M){\longrightarrow\,}X_{0}(N)
. Remembering that
D
is odd, we have
\mathcal{O}_{K}=\langle 1,\tau_{0}\rangle
with
\tau_{0}=\frac{-1+\sqrt{D}}{2}
. We choose the embedding
\sigma\colon H\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
and the ideal
\mathfrak{n}
such that
P_{\mathfrak{n}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{0}
, i.e.,
(A{\longrightarrow\,}A/A[\mathfrak{n}])
is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{C}/\mathcal{O}_{K}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{n}^{-1})
and
\mathfrak{n}^{-1}=\langle 1/N,\tau_{0}\rangle
. It is the Heegner point associated to
([\mathcal{O}_{K}],\mathfrak{n})
and
\mathfrak{n}=\langle N,\frac{1+\sqrt{D}}{2}\rangle
corresponds to
\beta=1\in\mathbb{Z}/2N\mathbb{Z}
, in the sense of Section
9.4
.

By definition, we have
A^{\mathfrak{a}}=A/A[\mathfrak{a}]
. We have
\mathcal{O}_{K}\subset\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
,
A[\mathfrak{a}]=\mathfrak{a}^{-1}/\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
A^{\mathfrak{a}}=\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
and
\phi^{\mathfrak{a}}\colon A{\longrightarrow\,}A^{\mathfrak{a}}
is the natural quotient isogeny
\mathbb{C}/\mathcal{O}_{K}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
. The isogeny
A^{\mathfrak{a}}{\longrightarrow\,}A^{\mathfrak{a}}/A^{\mathfrak{a}}[\mathfrak{n}]
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{-1}
. Thus, the Heegner point
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
corresponds via the description of Section
9.4
to
(\mathcal{A}^{-1},\mathfrak{n})
. Thus,
\mathfrak{a}^{-1}=\langle 1,\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}\rangle
with
\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\frac{-B+\sqrt{D}}{2A}\in\mathcal{H}
a solution to a quadratic equation

AX^{2}+BX+C,\qquad A>0,\qquad B^{2}-4AC=D,\qquad B\equiv\beta\pmod{2N},\qquad N\mid A.

If we write
\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}=x_{\mathfrak{a}}+iy_{\mathfrak{a}}
and
\tau_{0}=x_{0}+iy_{0}
, then

y_{\mathfrak{a}}=\frac{\sqrt{|D|}}{2A}=\frac{y_{0}}{A}.

Moreover,
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})=A
and
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

Lemma 10.4
.

We have

\cl_{P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}})=\frac{(-1)^{p}2^{p-2t}\sqrt{|D|}^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}^{1/2}}\frac{w^{-}(P^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{n}})}{\chi(\mathfrak{a})}\in\mathcal{W}_{P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}

and

\cl_{P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}(\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}})=\frac{(-1)^{p}2^{p-2t}\sqrt{|D|}^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}^{1/2}}\frac{w^{+}(P^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{n}})}{y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2t}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})}\in\mathcal{W}_{P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}.

Proof.

This follows by applying Proposition
10.3
with
y_{0}=\sqrt{|D|}/2
and using (
72
).
∎

11.
Archimedean local heights

In this section, we compute the archimedean local heights of generalized Heegner cycles using the connection (
24
) with Brylinski’s archimedean pairing.

11.1.
The case
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0

We maintain the notations of Section
10.2
.

Theorem 11.1
.

Let
(m,N)=1
,
\mathcal{A}_{1}=[\mathfrak{a}_{1}],\mathcal{A}_{2}=[\mathfrak{a}_{2}]\in\Cl_{K}
and assume that
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
and
T_{m}P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}
are disjoint in
X_{0}(N)
. Let
v
denote the infinite place of
H
corresponding to the fixed embedding
\sigma\colon H\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
of Section
10.2
. Then

\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t},

and

\langle\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})\sum_{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\bar{\tau}_{2})^{2t},

where
\tau_{i}:=\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}
so that
\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{-1}=\langle 1,\tau_{i}\rangle
and
P^{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}_{\mathfrak{n}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{i}
, for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

By definition of the Brylinski pairing, and by compatibility of the actions of Hecke correspondences on Chow groups with the action of Hecke operators on cohomology classes (
74
) (see
[
33
, Prop. 4.1.3]
), we have

\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}=\langle\cl_{P^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{n}}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}),T_{m}\cl_{P^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}_{\mathfrak{n}}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}.

The result follows by combining Lemma
10.4
and Proposition
9.5
.
∎

Let
\langle\;,\;\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}=\sum_{w|\infty}\langle\;,\;\rangle_{w}\epsilon_{w}
be the sum of the local heights on
X
over all the infinite places of
H
. Recall that we previously fixed a particular embedding
\sigma\colon H\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
in Section
10.2
corresponding to a place
v
. If
Z_{1},Z_{2}\in\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0}
are two cycles for which the height pairing
\langle Z_{1},Z_{2}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}
is defined (see Section
4
), and which lie in distinct fibers of
\pi\colon X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}
over
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
respectively, then
\langle Z_{1},Z_{2}\rangle_{w}=\langle\cl_{Q_{1}}(Z_{1,w}),\cl_{Q_{2}}(Z_{2,w})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}
, where
Z_{j,w}
is viewed as a cycle on the base-change
X_{w}=X\times_{w}\mathbb{C}
.

Theorem 11.2
.

Let
(m,N)=1
,
\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}
and assume that
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
. Then

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=-\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right),

\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=-\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right),

and

\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=0=\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}.

Proof.

The set of infinite places of
H
is in bijection with the class group
\Cl_{K}
of
K
. If
w
is an infinite place corresponding to the ideal class
\mathcal{B}\in\Cl_{K}
, then

(76)

\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{w}=\langle Z_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{A}_{1}},Z_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v},

for all
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}
. The proof of (
76
) is an adaption of
[
35
, Lem. 4.7]
.

Using (
76
), we see that

(77)

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=\sum_{w\mid\infty}\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{w}=\sum_{\mathcal{B}\in\Cl_{K}}\langle Z_{{\mathcal{B}}},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{v}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{i}=[\mathfrak{a}_{i}]
with
\mathfrak{a}_{i}
an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
i=1,2
.
The condition
r_{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}}(m)=0
implies that
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
and
T_{m}P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}
are disjoint in
X_{0}(N)
. We can therefore apply Theorem
11.1
to obtain

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}.

Recall that for
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in R_{N}^{m}
, we have
\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=c\bar{\tau}_{1}\tau_{2}+d\bar{\tau}_{1}-a\tau_{2}-b
with
\tau_{i}=\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}
such that
\langle 1,\tau_{i}\rangle=\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{-1}
. In particular,
\bar{\tau}_{1}\in\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}^{-1}
and
\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})^{-1}
. Let
\mathfrak{a}=\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2}
. This is an integral ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
such that
[\mathfrak{a}]=\mathcal{A}
and we have
\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
. For any positive integer
j
, we have

r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(j):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K},[\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}]=\mathcal{A}\\
N(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})=j\end{subarray}}\chi(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}).

Every ideal
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
satisfying
[\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}]=\mathcal{A}
can be written as
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=x\mathfrak{a}
for some
x\in K
. The fact that
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
is an integral ideal translates into the requirement
x\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
. Note that
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})=A_{1}A_{2}
. Thus, we obtain

(78)

r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(j)=\frac{1}{\#\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\\
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(x)=\frac{j}{A_{1}A_{2}}\end{subarray}}\chi(x\mathfrak{a})=\frac{\chi(\mathfrak{a})}{\#\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\\
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(x)=\frac{j}{A_{1}A_{2}}\end{subarray}}x^{2t}.

We used the fact that
\chi
is a homomorphism
I_{K}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
with the property
\chi((\alpha))=\alpha^{2t}
(since
\chi
is assumed to be unramified). In particular,
\chi((x\alpha))=\alpha^{2t}
for any unit
x\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.

Recall that

(79)

g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})=-Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|\tau_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2}|^{2}}{2y_{1}\Im(\gamma\tau_{2})}\right).

Define
\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\tau_{2})=c\tau_{1}\tau_{2}+d\tau_{1}-a\tau_{2}-b
and note that

\frac{|\tau_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2}|^{2}}{2y_{1}\Im(\gamma\tau_{2})}=\frac{2A_{1}A_{2}|\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})|^{2}}{|D|\det(\gamma)}=\frac{2A_{1}A_{2}N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}))}{|D|\det(\gamma)}.

The elements
\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
and
\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
are the same as the ones denoted
\alpha
and
\beta
in
[
22
, II (3.6)]
except that the roles of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
are swapped. We have

\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})^{-1}=\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\in(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})^{-1}\mathfrak{n},

and we set

n=\frac{A_{1}A_{2}}{N}N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})).

This is an integer by the same calculation as in
[
22
, II (3.5)]
(the only difference being that the roles of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
are swapped there).
Moreover, we have

N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}))=\frac{m|D|+nN}{A_{1}A_{2}},

as in
[
22
, II (3.9)]
.
With these notations, we have

(80)

g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})=-Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right).

Define

\rho^{m}_{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}}(n):=\#\{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\mid A_{1}A_{2}N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}))=nN\}.

According to
[
22
, II (3.9)]
, the association
\gamma\mapsto(\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}),\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}))
gives a bijection from
\{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\mid A_{1}A_{2}N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}))=nN\}
to the set

\Xi_{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}}(n):=\left\{(\alpha,\beta)\in(\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\times(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})^{-1}\mathfrak{n})/\pm 1\mid N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha)=\frac{m|D|+nN}{A_{1}A_{2}},\right.\\
\left.N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\beta)=\frac{nN}{A_{1}A_{2}},A_{1}A_{2}\alpha\equiv A_{1}A_{2}\beta\pmod{\mathfrak{d}}\right\}.

Putting all this together yields

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}=-\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\chi(\mathfrak{a})\sum_{(\alpha,\beta)\in\Xi_{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}}(n)}\alpha^{2t}.

In view of (
78
) and the proof of
[
22
, II (3.16)]
, we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\chi(\mathfrak{a})\sum_{(\alpha,\beta)\in\Xi_{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}}(n)}\alpha^{2t}=u^{2}\delta(n)R_{\{\bar{\mathcal{A}}[\mathfrak{n}]\}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|+nN).

Here,
R_{\{\bar{\mathcal{A}}[\mathfrak{n}]\}}(n)
is the number of integral ideals of norm
n
in the genus of
\bar{\mathcal{A}}\mathfrak{n}
,
\delta(n)
is
2^{\omega((n,D))}
, and
u=|\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}|/2
. By
[
22
, IV (4.6)]
, we have
\sigma_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)=\delta(n)R_{\{\bar{\mathcal{A}}[\mathfrak{n}]\}}(n)
. We deduce the final formula

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}=-\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right).

The calculation for
\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}
can be checked similarly.
∎

11.2.
The case
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)\neq 0

In this section, we prove the following generalization of Theorem
11.2
, valid even in the case of improper intersection:

Theorem 11.3
.

Given
\mathcal{A}=[\mathfrak{a}]\in\Cl_{K}
and
(m,N)=1
, we have

\displaystyle\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\left[-u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+huD^{t}r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k+t)+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k-t)-2\log(2\pi)+2\frac{L^{\prime}}{L}(1,\epsilon_{K})+\log(|D|)\right)\right],

\displaystyle\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\left[-u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+huD^{t}r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m)\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k+t)+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k-t)-2\log(2\pi)+2\frac{L^{\prime}}{L}(1,\epsilon_{K})+\log(|D|)\right)\right],

and

\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=0=\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}.

We will give a detailed proof of the formula for
\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}
. The one for
\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}
can be deduced similarly and is left to the reader.

11.2.1.
Gross and Zagier’s modification

We begin by reviewing the modifications required in
[
22
]
, where the height pairing is between divisors of degree zero on
X_{0}(N)
. Let
a
and
b
be two divisors of degree zero with common support equal to a point
x\in X_{0}(N)(\mathbb{C})
. For every point
y\in X_{0}(N)(\mathbb{C})
near
x
but not in the support of
b
, define
a_{y}
to be the divisor obtained from
a
by replacing every occurrence of
x
by
y
. In other words, if
a=\sum_{z\neq x}m_{z}(z)+m_{x}(x)
, then
a_{y}=\sum_{z\neq x}m_{z}(z)+m_{x}(y)
. Then the local intersection
\langle a_{y},b\rangle_{v}
is well-defined and can be used to approximate
\langle a,b\rangle_{v}
. More precisely, let
t_{0}
be a uniformizing parameter for
x
on
X_{0}(N)
, i.e., a function such that
\ord_{x}(t_{0})=1
. Then Gross defined in
[
21
]
the local archimedean height pairing

(81)

\langle a,b\rangle_{v}:=\lim\limits_{y\rightarrow x}(\langle a_{y},b\rangle_{v}-\ord_{x}(a)\ord_{x}(b)\log|t_{0}(y)|_{v})

and showed that, with an appropriate definition of local non-archimedean height pairings,
\sum_{v}\langle a,b\rangle_{v}
is independent of the choice of
t_{0}
and equal to the global Néron–Tate height pairing
\langle a,b\rangle^{\mathrm{NT}}
(which is well-defined on linear equivalence classes of divisors even if there is common support since one can use a moving lemma).

If
x
is a Heegner point,
c=(x)-(\infty)
,
d=(x)-(0)
,
(m,N)=1
, and
\sigma=\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
, then
\ord_{x}(T_{m}d^{\sigma})=r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)
. Hence, when
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)\neq 0
, the common support of
c
and
T_{m}d^{\sigma}
is non-empty and equal to
x
. Applying (
81
) yields

\langle c,T_{m}d^{\sigma}\rangle_{v}=\lim\limits_{y\rightarrow x}(\langle(y)-(\infty),T_{m}d^{\sigma}\rangle_{v}-r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)\log|t_{0}(y)|_{v}).

The uniformizing parameter in
[
22
]
is chosen as follows: consider the differential

\omega=\eta^{4}(z)\frac{dq}{q}=2\pi i\eta^{4}(z)dz,

where
\eta(z)=q^{1/24}\prod_{n}(1-q^{n})
is the Dedekind eta function.
The differential
\omega
is only well-defined up to sixth roots of unity, but this does not affect the later calculations. Letting
u(x)=|\Aut(x)|/2
, we have

\ord_{x}(\omega)=\frac{1}{u(x)}-1,

and
\omega
may be normalized such that in a neighborhood of
x
it takes the form

\omega=(1+O(t_{0}))t_{0}^{\frac{1}{u(x)}}\frac{dt_{0}}{t_{0}}.

This normalization ensures that for a complex place
v
, we have

(82)

\lim\limits_{y\rightarrow x}(\log|t_{0}(y)|_{v}-u(x)\log|2\pi i\eta^{4}(z)(w-z)|_{v})=0,

where
x=\Gamma_{0}(N)z
and
y=\Gamma_{0}(N)w
. Setting
t_{1}=t_{0}^{1/u(x)}
yields a uniformizing parameter for
\Gamma(M)z
on
X(M)
.

11.2.2.
Archimedean self-intersections of generalized Heegner cycles over
X(M)

Let
\mathcal{A}=[\mathfrak{a}]\in\Cl_{K}
and let
v
denote our fixed archimedean place. The aim of this section is to compute

\left\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}},Z_{\mathcal{A}}\right\rangle_{v}
.

Define

\mathcal{G}_{1}(z,z^{\prime}):=\frac{4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}(y^{\prime})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}(N)}j(\gamma,z)^{-2t}\mu^{-}_{g}(\gamma z,z^{\prime}),

which makes sense for any
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\Gamma(M)z\neq\Gamma(M)z^{\prime}
. We then have

\mathcal{G}_{1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}},\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{2}})=\left\langle\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}},\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}\right\rangle_{v}

by Theorem
9.1
and Proposition
10.3
, whenever
\Gamma(M)\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\neq\Gamma(M)\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}
.

Proposition 11.4
.

We have

\left\langle\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}},\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}\right\rangle_{v}=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}},w)+(-1)^{k+t}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})_{\pi^{-1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})}\log|t_{1}(w)|_{v}\right).

Proof.

We apply Theorem
4.3
to
X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}=X(M)
. Indeed, the set of Heegner points with CM by orders
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
is dense in
X_{0}(N)
, and the corresponding set of preimages of such points under
\pi_{M,N}\colon\bar{C}{\longrightarrow\,}X_{0}(N)
is dense. In the fiber of
\pi\colon X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}
above each such point there is a generalized Heegner cycle whose cycle class provides the desired cohomology class in Theorem
4.3
corresponding to
\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})
. When
\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}_{K}
, it follows from Lemma
10.4
that these Hodge classes span the fiber of
\mathcal{W}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}
above that point. The general case follows via isogeny.
∎

Remark 11.5
.

The self-intersection of
\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}
in the fiber above
\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}
is with respect to the intersection form
(\;,\;)_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}
on
H_{\dR}^{2k+2t-2}(\pi^{-1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}))
defined by

(\alpha,\beta)_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}=\int_{\pi^{-1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})}\alpha\wedge\beta.

More precisely, we have

(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})_{\pi^{-1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})}=(\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}),\overline{\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})})_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}=(-1)^{k+t-1}\langle\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}),\cl_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})\rangle_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}.

Recalling that
p=k-1
, we deduce that

(\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}})_{\pi^{-1}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})}=(-1)^{p+t}\frac{4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}}\langle w^{-}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}),w^{-}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}=(-1)^{p+t}\frac{4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2t}}

by Proposition
10.3
. This agrees with the Hodge Index Theorem
[
37
, Thm. 6.33]
, which implies that the signature of
(\;,\;)_{\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}
restricted to a primitive class of type
(p+t,p+t)
is non-trivial of signature
(-1)^{p+t}
. See also Zhang’s remark
[
41
, p. 123]
.

Putting everything together yields the formula

\left\langle\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}},\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}\right\rangle_{v}=\frac{4^{k-2t-1}|D|^{k-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2t}}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma(M)}g(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}},\gamma w)\left(\frac{\bar{\tau}_{\mathfrak{a}}-\gamma w}{2iy_{\mathfrak{a}}}\right)^{2t}j(\gamma,w)^{2t}-\log|t_{1}(w)|_{v}\right).

11.2.3.
Archimedean self-intersections of generalized Heegner cycles over
X_{0}(N)

Let
t_{0}
be a uniformizing parameter for
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}
on
X_{0}(N)
, chosen with the same normalization as in
[
22
]
. Let
u_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=u(P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}})
.

Proposition 11.6
.

We have

\left\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}},Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\right\rangle_{v}=\frac{|S|2^{2k-4t-2}|D|^{k-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{2t}}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}}\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}g(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}},\gamma w)\left(\frac{\bar{\tau}_{\mathfrak{a}}-\gamma w}{2iy_{\mathfrak{a}}}\right)^{2t}j(\gamma,w)^{2t}-\log|t_{0}(w)|_{v}\right).

Proof.

Write
Q_{i}=\Gamma(M)\tau_{i}
for the points of
X(M)
sitting above
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
. Write
Z_{i}=\epsilon Y_{Q_{i}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
. Just as in the proof of Theorem
9.3
, we have

|S|^{-1}\left\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}},Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\right\rangle_{v}=\left\langle\sum_{i}Z_{i},Z_{1}\right\rangle_{v}=\langle Z_{1},Z_{1}\rangle_{v}+\sum_{i\neq 1}\langle Z_{i},Z_{1}\rangle_{v}.

Setting
t_{1}=t_{0}^{\frac{1}{u_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}}
gives a uniformizing parameter for
Q_{1}
on
X(M)
.
We then have

\displaystyle|S|^{-1}\left\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}},Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\right\rangle_{v}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\tau_{1}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}(\tau_{1},w)+(-1)^{k+t}(Z_{1}\cdot Z_{1})_{\pi^{-1}(\tau_{1})}u_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\log|t_{1}(w)|_{v}\right)+\sum_{i\neq 1}\mathcal{G}_{1}(\tau_{i},\tau_{1})

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\tau_{1}}\left(\sum_{i}\mathcal{G}_{1}(\tau_{i},w)+(-1)^{k+t}(Z_{1}\cdot Z_{1})_{\pi^{-1}(\tau_{1})}\log|t_{0}(w)|_{v}\right).

The formula then follows as in the proof of Theorem
9.3
.
∎

11.2.4.
Archimedean local heights of generalized Heegner cycles

Given
(m,N)=1
and
\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}
we ultimately wish to compute
\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}
and
\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}
in the case
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)\neq 0
.
Let
v
be our fixed archimedean place as chosen above. Let
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}
and let
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}
. For the sake of notation, we write
\tau_{i}=\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{i}}
.

The following elementary observation will be useful in proving the next theorem.

Lemma 11.7
.

Let
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in R_{N}
with
\det(\gamma)=m
. Assume that
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
. Then

|j(\gamma,\tau_{2})|^{2}=my_{2}y_{1}^{-1}=m\frac{A_{1}}{A_{2}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad|\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})|^{2}=4my_{1}y_{2}=\frac{m|D|}{A_{1}A_{2}}.

Proof.

Since
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
, we have in particular
\Im(\gamma\tau_{2})=\Im(\tau_{1})
, i.e.,

\frac{my_{2}}{|c\tau_{2}+d|^{2}}=y_{1}.

The first equality follows since
j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=c\tau_{2}+d
. The second equality follows from the first after noticing that

\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=(\bar{\tau}_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=-2iy_{1}j(\gamma,\tau_{2}),

since
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
.
∎

The following result generalizes Theorem
11.1
.

Theorem 11.8
.

Given
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}

\displaystyle,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}\neq\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}\left(\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\right)g(\tau_{1},w)-\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\right)\log|t_{0}(w)|_{v}\right)\right].

Proof.

We set
p=k-1
. We compute

\displaystyle\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},

\displaystyle T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}=\left\langle\cl_{P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}),\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}\gamma\cdot\cl_{P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\left\langle w^{-}(P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}),\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}\gamma\cdot w^{-}(P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\left\langle w^{-}(P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}),\sum_{\gamma\in\pm\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})^{-2t}w^{-}(\gamma P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{2}})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})^{-2t}\left\langle w^{-}(\tau_{1}),w^{-}(\gamma\tau_{2})\right\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\frac{m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}\neq\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})^{-2t}\left\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}},Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\right\rangle_{v}.

Using Proposition
11.6
, we then obtain

\displaystyle\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},

\displaystyle T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\rangle_{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|4^{p-2t}|D|^{p}}{\binom{2p}{p-t}\chi(\mathfrak{a}_{1})\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{2})}\left[\frac{m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}\neq\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})^{-2t}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}\left(\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}w)\left(\frac{\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},w)}{2iy_{1}\Im(w)}\right)^{2t}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{y_{1}^{2t}}\log|t_{0}(w)|_{v}\right)\right].

Next, we consider the term

(83)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})^{-2t}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}w)\left(\frac{\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},w)}{2iy_{1}\Im(w)}\right)^{2t}.

We begin by replacing the limit to
\tau_{1}
with a limit to
\tau_{2}
by replacing
w
by
\gamma w
, yielding

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}m^{p+t}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})^{-2t}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{2}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma w)\left(\frac{\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},\gamma w)}{2iy_{1}\Im(\gamma w)}\right)^{2t}.

By Lemma
11.7
, we see that

j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})^{-1}=j(\gamma,\tau_{2})m^{-1}y_{1}y_{2}^{-1},

and we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{m^{p-t}y_{1}^{2t}}{y_{2}^{2t}}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})^{2t}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{2}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma w)\left(\frac{\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},\gamma w)}{2iy_{1}\Im(\gamma w)}\right)^{2t}.

This is in turn equal to

\frac{m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{2}}\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma w)\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},\gamma w)^{2t}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})^{2t}.

Next, observe that

\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{2}}\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},\gamma w)j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=(\bar{\tau_{1}}-\gamma^{\prime}\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma,\tau_{2})\\
=(\bar{\tau_{1}}-\gamma^{\prime}\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{2})=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}).

We deduce that the desired limit (
83
) is equal to

\frac{m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\right)\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}g(\tau_{1},w)\\
+\frac{m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1\\
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}\neq\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}g(\tau_{1},\gamma^{\prime}\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}.

It remains to prove the equality

(84)

\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{m^{p+t}}{(y_{1}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2}))^{2t}}\log|t_{0}(\gamma w)|_{v}\\
=\frac{m^{p-t}}{(2iy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\right)\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}\log|t(w)|_{v}.

We use the equality

\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\bar{\tau_{2}})=(\tau_{1}-\gamma\bar{\tau}_{2})j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})=2iy_{1}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2})

in order to substitute
(y_{1}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{2}))^{-2t}=(2i)^{2t}\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\bar{\tau_{2}})^{-2t}
. Using Lemma
11.7
, we make the substitution

\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\bar{\tau_{2}})^{-1}=\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})|\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})|^{-2}=\frac{\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}{4my_{1}y_{2}}.

We obtain,

(y_{1}j(\gamma,\bar{\tau_{2}}))^{-2t}=\frac{(2i)^{2t}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}}{(4my_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}=\frac{\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}}{(2imy_{1}y_{2})^{2t}}.

Equation (
84
) easily follows.
∎

Theorem 11.9
.

Given
\mathcal{A}=[\mathfrak{a}]\in\Cl_{K}
and
(m,N)=1
, and letting
p=k-1
, we have

\displaystyle\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\left[-u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+uD^{t}r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}(g(\tau_{1},w)-\log|2\pi i\eta^{4}(\tau_{1})(w-\tau_{1})|_{v})\right],

Proof.

We set
p=k-1
.
We proceed as in the proof of Theorem (
11.2
): letting
\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=\alpha(\gamma,\bar{\tau}_{1},\tau_{2})=(\tau_{1}-\gamma\tau_{2})j(\gamma,\tau_{2})
, we set

n:=\frac{A_{1}A_{2}}{N}N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\beta(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})),

which is a non-negative integer. We remark that
n=0
if and only if
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
. In particular, when
\gamma\tau_{2}\neq\tau_{1}
, we have
n>0
and the exact same proof as for Theorem
11.2
shows that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}\neq\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}g(\tau_{1},\gamma\tau_{2})\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\\
=-\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{p-t}}{D^{t}\binom{2p}{p-t}}u^{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right).

Next, we claim that

\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}=D^{t}ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m).

Indeed, when
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
we have the simple expression

\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=-2iy_{1}(c\tau_{2}+d)=-\frac{\sqrt{D}}{A_{1}}j(\gamma,\tau_{2}).

Therefore, we have

\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}=D^{t}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}(A_{1}^{-1}j(\gamma,\tau_{2}))^{2t}.

Let
\mathfrak{a}=\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2}
. We claim that the assignment
\gamma\mapsto\eta(\gamma):=A_{1}^{-1}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})
gives a bijection between

S_{1}:=\{\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\mid\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\}

and

S_{2}:=\{\eta\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}/\pm 1\mid N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\eta)=m/N(\mathfrak{a})\}.

Recall that
\mathfrak{a}_{i}=\langle A_{i},(B_{i}+\sqrt{D})/2\rangle=A_{i}\langle 1,\bar{\tau}_{i}\rangle
and
\mathfrak{a}_{i}^{-1}=\langle 1,\tau_{i}\rangle
.
Because
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
, we then have

\eta(\gamma)=A_{1}^{-1}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=A_{1}^{-1}\tau_{1}^{-1}(a\tau_{2}+b)\in A_{1}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2}^{-1}=(A_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})^{-1}=(\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}\mathfrak{a}_{2})^{-1}=\mathfrak{a}^{-1}.

Using Lemma
11.7
, we see that

N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\eta(\gamma))=A_{1}^{-2}|j(\gamma,\tau_{2})|^{2}=A_{1}^{-2}m\frac{A_{1}}{A_{2}}=\frac{m}{A_{1}A_{2}}=\frac{m}{N(\mathfrak{a})}.

We conclude that
\eta(\gamma)\in S_{2}
for all
\gamma\in S_{1}
. Suppose that
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in S_{1}
satisfy
\eta(\gamma)=\eta(\gamma^{\prime})
. Then
j(\gamma,\tau_{2})=j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{2})
. In particular,
(c-c^{\prime})\tau_{2}=d^{\prime}-d
, which implies that
(c-c^{\prime})y_{2}=0
, hence
c=c^{\prime}
and thus
d=d^{\prime}
. We conclude that the assignment
\gamma\mapsto\eta(\gamma)
is injective. There are
ur_{\mathcal{A}}(m)
elements
\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1
satisfying
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}

[
22
, p. 251]
. Since

\#S_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\#\left\{\eta\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\mid N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\eta)=\frac{m}{N(\mathfrak{a})}\right\}=\frac{\#\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}{2}r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=ur_{\mathcal{A}}(m),

we deduce that
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are indeed in bijection.

We deduce that

\chi(\mathfrak{a})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}=D^{t}\frac{\chi(\mathfrak{a})}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\\
N(\eta)=m/N(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}}\eta^{2t}=D^{t}ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m),

where we used (
78
).

To finish the proof, we must argue that

\chi(\mathfrak{a})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}=D^{t}r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m).

Suppose that
\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}
satisfies
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
and that there exists
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1
such that
\gamma^{\prime}\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}
. Then
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}
and

\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=-2iy_{1}j(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{2})=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=-2iy_{1}j(\gamma,\tau_{2})j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})=\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1}).

We have

D^{t}ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)=\chi(\mathfrak{a})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}=\chi(\mathfrak{a})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash R_{N}^{m}\\
\gamma\tau_{2}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})^{2t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1\\
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})^{2t}.

The problem thus reduces to showing that

(85)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1\\
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})^{2t}=u.

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1\\
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})^{2t}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)/\pm 1\\
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}\end{subarray}}\chi((j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1}))).

If
u=1
, then only
\gamma^{\prime}=1
appears and
j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})=1
so that (
85
) is satisfied trivially. Assume now that
u>1
.
The condition
\gamma^{\prime}\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}
implies that
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1}))=1
. If
j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
, then
j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
so
\chi((j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})))=1
and we are done because the sum has size
u
. Note that

j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})=\tau_{1}^{-1}(a\tau_{1}+b).

We have
a\tau_{1}+b\in\mathfrak{a}_{1}^{-1}
, so if
\tau_{1}^{-1}\in\mathfrak{a}_{1}
, then
j(\gamma^{\prime},\tau_{1})\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Thus, it suffices to check that
\tau_{1}^{-1}\in\mathfrak{a}_{1}
.
If
u=2
, then
K=\mathbb{Q}(i)
and
\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{1}\in X_{0}(N)
is a lift of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})i\in X_{0}(1)
. We may thus assume that
\tau_{1}=i
. If
u=3
, then
K=\mathbb{Q}(\rho)
where
\rho=e^{2\pi i/3}
and we may assume
\tau_{1}=\rho
. In either case, we have
|\tau_{1}|^{2}=1
and
A_{1}=1
, hence
\tau_{1}^{-1}=\bar{\tau}_{1}\in\mathfrak{a}_{1}=A_{1}\langle 1,\bar{\tau}_{1}\rangle
. This concludes the proof.

The logarithmic term is dealt with by using (
82
).
∎

We deal with the limit that appears in Theorem
11.9
:

Proposition 11.10
.

We have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\lim\limits_{w\to\tau_{1}}(g(\tau_{1},w)-\log|2\pi i\eta^{4}(\tau_{1})(w-\tau_{1})|_{v})\\
=h\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k+t)+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k-t)-2\log(2\pi)+2\frac{L^{\prime}}{L}(1,\epsilon_{K})+\log(|D|)\right).

Proof.

The proof is the same as the proof of
[
22
, II (5.8)]
, except that we use Lemma
2.5
.
∎

Combining Proposition
11.10
with Theorem
11.9
completes the proof of Theorem
11.3
.

12.
Non-archimedean local heights

In this section we compute the non-archimedean contribution to the height pairing of generalized Heegner cycles. More precisely, we derive a formula for

\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}:=\sum_{v\nmid\infty}\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{v}\epsilon_{v}.

The analogous formula for
\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}
can be deduced by noting, as in
[
34
, bottom p. 75]
, that

(86)

\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}=\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}.

12.1.
The case
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0

Let
v
be a non-archimedean place above a prime
q
. Let
m\geq 1
and assume that
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
. Let
L=H_{v}
denote the completion at
v
and let
\Lambda
be the ring of integers of the maximal unramified extension
L^{\mathrm{ur}}
of
L
, with residue field
\mathbb{F}=\bar{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Let
\underline{X}_{0}(N){\longrightarrow\,}\spec(\mathbb{Z})
be the Katz–Mazur integral model
[
26
]
and denote by
\underline{X}_{0}(N)_{\Lambda}{\longrightarrow\,}\spec(\Lambda)
the pull-back with special fiber
\underline{X}_{0}(N)_{\mathbb{F}}{\longrightarrow\,}\spec(\mathbb{F})=s
and generic fiber
X_{0}(N)_{L^{\mathrm{ur}}}{\longrightarrow\,}\spec(L^{\mathrm{ur}})=\eta
. Let
\iota
denote the inclusion
Y_{0}(N)\times_{\mathbb{Q}}F^{\mathrm{ur}}\hookrightarrow\underline{X}_{0}(N)_{\Lambda}
.

Choose a prime
p
that splits in
K
and that is distinct from
q
. Consider the following smooth
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-sheaf on
Y(M)
:

\mathcal{W}_{p}:=\sym^{2k-2}(R^{1}\pi_{\mathcal{E},*}\mathbb{Q}_{p})(k-1)\otimes\kappa_{2t}H^{2t}(\bar{A}^{2t},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(t)),

where
\pi_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}{\longrightarrow\,}Y(M)
is the universal elliptic curve.
The cycle
\epsilon Y^{\mathfrak{a}}_{Q}\in\CH^{k+t}(X_{F})_{0,K}
is supported in the fiber of
\pi_{X}\colon X{\longrightarrow\,}\bar{C}=X(M)
over
Q^{\mathfrak{a}}\in Y(M)(F)
, which is identified with
(A_{F}^{\mathfrak{a}})^{2k-2}\times_{F}A_{F}^{2t}
. Its image under the
p
-adic cycle class map on
\pi_{X}^{-1}(Q^{\mathfrak{a}})
is denoted

b(Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}})\in\epsilon H_{\et}^{2k+2t-2}\left(\pi_{X}^{-1}(Q^{\mathfrak{a}})_{\bar{F}},\mathbb{Q}_{p}(k+t-1)\right)\simeq H^{0}(\overline{Q^{\mathfrak{a}}},\mathcal{W}_{p}),

and in fact lies in
H^{0}(\overline{Q^{\mathfrak{a}}},\mathcal{W}_{p})^{G_{F}}
.
This is the Tate vector associated with the cycle
\epsilon Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}}
. Taking the
p
-adic cycle class map with respect to
\pi_{X}^{-1}(Q^{\mathfrak{a}})
of the cycle
\bar{\epsilon}Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}}
yields a Tate vector that we denote by
\bar{b}(Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}})
. These Tate vectors completely determine the
p
-adic Abel–Jacobi images of the respective cycles with respect to the variety
X
. Recall the quotient map
\pi=\pi_{M,N}\colon Y(M){\longrightarrow\,}Y_{0}(N)=Y(M)/\penalty S
and let
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}:=(\pi_{*}\mathcal{W}_{p})^{S}
, a
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-sheaf on
Y_{0}(N)
. Then
a(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})=\sum_{Q\in\pi^{-1}(P_{\mathfrak{n}})}b(Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}})
and
\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})=\sum_{Q\in\pi^{-1}(P_{\mathfrak{n}})}\bar{b}(Y_{Q}^{\mathfrak{a}})
are Tate vectors with respect to the sheaf
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}
defined over
H
.

Following
[
35
]
, suppose
c
and
d
are Tate vectors with respect to the sheaf
\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p}
supported at points
y_{c}\neq y_{d}
of
Y_{0}(N)(L^{\mathrm{ur}})
of good reduction. Let
\underline{y}_{c}
and
\underline{y}_{d}
be the Zariski closures of the points
y_{c}
and
y_{d}
in
X_{0}(N)_{\Lambda}
, and let
\underline{c}
and
\underline{d}
be extensions of
c
and
d
to
H^{0}(\underline{y}_{c},\iota_{*}\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p})
and
H^{0}(\underline{y}_{d},\iota_{*}\mathcal{W}^{0}_{p})
respectively. If
\underline{y}_{c}
and
\underline{y}_{d}
have common special fiber
z\in\underline{X}_{0}(N)_{\mathbb{F}}
corresponding to an elliptic curve
E/\penalty\mathbb{F}
, then define

(c,d)_{v}:=(\underline{y}_{c}\cdot\underline{y}_{d})_{z}\cdot(\underline{c}_{z},\underline{d}_{z}),

where
(\underline{y}_{c}\cdot\underline{y}_{d})_{z}
is the usual intersection number at
z
on the arithmetic surface
\underline{X}_{0}(N)_{\Lambda}
and
(\underline{c}_{z},\underline{d}_{z})
is the intersection pairing on the
p
-adic étale cohomology of
E^{2k-2}\times A_{\mathbb{F}}^{2t}
.

The assumption
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
guarantees that the Tate vectors
a(Y)
and
T_{m}a(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})
have disjoint supports. Moreover, we may assume that they are supported at points of good reduction (because CM elliptic curves have potential good reduction). The number
(a(Y),T_{m}a(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}
is thus defined. The cycle
Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
sits above the Heegner point
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{\mathfrak{a}}\in Y_{0}(N)(H)
. Letting
\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
denote the Zariski closure of
P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
in
\underline{X}_{0}(N)_{\Lambda}
, the cycle
Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
admits an extension
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
sitting in the fiber of
\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
. The intersection cycle
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}\cdot T_{m}\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
has a flat presentation over the common support
|\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}}|\cap|T_{m}\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}|
by
[
41
, Lem. 3.3.2]
and the fact that the cycles have presentations by abelian subvarieties. Thus, following
[
41
, (3.3.1)]
, we obtain

\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}\cdot T_{m}\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=(-1)^{k+t}(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}.

We conclude that

(87)

\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\rangle_{v}\epsilon_{v}=(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}\log N(v).

Remark 12.1
.

Under the assumption
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
, the local Brylinski pairing at
v
of the vectors
a(Y)
and
T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})
is defined and, according to
[
10
, Appendix]
, we have

\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\rangle_{v}\epsilon_{v}=\langle a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}_{v},

where the local height on the left hand side is the one of Gillet–Soulé.
Brylinski moreover gives an explicit description of his local pairing under the assumption that
H^{2}(\underline{Y}_{0}(N)_{\mathbb{F}},\mathcal{W}_{p}^{0}(1))=0
, namely

\langle a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})\rangle^{\mathrm{Br}}_{v}=(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}\log N(v).

The proof that
H^{2}(\underline{Y}_{0}(N)_{\mathbb{F}},\mathcal{W}_{p}^{0}(1))=0
can be found in
[
35
, Proof of Prop. 5.1]
.

We have seen that the calculation of
\langle Z_{\mathfrak{n}},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\rangle_{v}\epsilon_{v}
boils down to calculating
(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}
. The latter has been calculated already in
[
35
]
:

Proposition 12.2
.

Let
m\geq 1
such that
(m,N)=1
and
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
. Then

(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}\\
=\frac{|S|}{2}m^{k-t-1}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda/\penalty\pi^{n}}(\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}})_{m}}\left(\bar{\epsilon}\left(X^{\otimes k-t-1}_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}\otimes X^{\otimes 2t}_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}\right),\epsilon\left(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}\right)\right).

Proof.

This is
[
35
, Prop. 5.2]
, except for the factor of
|S|
due to our normalizations. For future use, we summarize the proof. In order to do this, we switch to the notations used in
[
35
]
and
[
31
]
.

We let
x=a(Y)
and
\bar{x}^{\mathfrak{a}}=\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}})
. These Tate vectors sit above the points
y=P_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
y^{\sigma}=P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
in
X_{0}(N)
, where
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
corresponds to
[\mathfrak{a}]\in\Cl_{K}
. We denote by
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{y},\underline{y}^{\sigma}
the extensions over
\Lambda
, and by
\underline{x}_{s},(\underline{x}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{s},\underline{y}_{s},(\underline{y}^{\sigma})_{s}
the corresponding special fibers.

Let
q
be the prime below
v
and write
m=m_{0}q^{s}
with
(m_{0},q)=1
. Following
[
22
, III §5]
, we have

T_{m_{0}}\underline{y}^{\sigma}=\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g},

and

T_{q^{s}}\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g}=\sum_{j}\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g}(j).

The latter sum is indexed by certain integers between

0
and
s
, and the
\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g}(j)
are divisors over
\Lambda
comprised of quasi-canonical liftings of level
p^{j}
of their reductions (the notations are borrowed from
[
22
, III (5.2)]
).

By definition of the Hecke operators, we have

T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}\sum_{j}m^{k-t-1}\overline{\underline{x}}_{g}^{\mathfrak{a}}(j),

where
\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}}(j)
is a Tate vector sitting above
\underline{y}^{\mathfrak{a}}(j)
. In the case where
\underline{y}
and
\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma}(j)
intersect, the special fiber of
\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}}(j)
is represented by
[
35
, p. 1150]

(88)

(\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}}(j))_{s}=(\underline{y}_{s},\bar{\epsilon}(X_{hg\sqrt{D}g^{-1}h^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{hg\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t})),

for any
h\in\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}
with
n\geq 1
. Note that this special fiber does not depend on
j
.

We deduce that

\displaystyle|S|^{-1}(\underline{x},

\displaystyle T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{v}=\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}\sum_{j}m^{k-t-1}(\underline{x},\overline{\underline{x}}_{g}^{\mathfrak{a}}(j))

\displaystyle=m^{k-t-1}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}\sum_{j}(\underline{y}\cdot\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma}(j))(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{hg\sqrt{D}g^{-1}h^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{hg\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))

\displaystyle=m^{k-t-1}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}(\underline{y}\cdot T_{q^{s}}\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma})(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{hg\sqrt{D}g^{-1}h^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{hg\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t})).

By
[
22
, III (4.4)]
, we have

(\underline{y}\cdot T_{q^{s}}\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}}1.

Injecting this into the above expression, we obtain

\displaystyle(\underline{x},

\displaystyle T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{h\in\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{hg\sqrt{D}g^{-1}h^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{hg\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t})),

where the first equality holds because (
88
) is true for any
h
.
∎

The right hand side of the formula of Proposition
12.2
is
0
when the prime
q
splits in
K
by
[
22
, III (7.1)]
(which shows that the double sum is empty). When
q
is non-split,
\End_{\Lambda/\penalty\pi}(\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}})=R
is an order in the quaternion algebra
B
ramified at
\infty
and
q
. In this case, the double sum above can be made more explicit. The reduction of endomorphisms induces an embedding
K\hookrightarrow B
, which yields a decomposition

B=B_{+}\oplus B_{-}=K\oplus Kj,

where
j
is an element in the non-trivial coset of
N_{B^{\times}}(K^{\times})/\penalty K^{\times}
. The reduced norm on
B
is additive with respect to this decomposition. Every
b\in B
will be written as
b=\alpha+b_{-}=\alpha+\beta j
, and we then have
N(b)=N(\alpha)+N(b_{-})=N(\alpha)+N(\beta j)
.

Proposition 12.3
.

Let
m\geq 1
such that
(m,N)=1
and
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
. Then

(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}=|S|m^{k-t-1}\frac{(4D)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=\alpha+b_{-}\in R\mathfrak{a}/\penalty\pm 1\\
N(b)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}U(b_{-})P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2N(b_{-})}{N(b)}\right),

where

U(b_{-})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }q\text{ splits in }K\\
\frac{1}{2}(1+\ord_{q}(N(b_{-})))&\text{ if }q\text{ is inert in }K\\
\ord_{q}(|D|N(b_{-}))&\text{ if }q\text{ is ramified in }K,\end{cases}

and
P_{k,t}(x)
is the Jacobi polynomial introduced in (
11
).

Proof.

By the previous discussion, we may assume that
q
is non-split in
K
.
According to
[
22
, III (7.3)]
, we then have

\End_{W/\penalty\pi^{n}}(\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}})=\{b=\alpha+b_{-}\in R\colon|D|N(b_{-})\equiv 0\mod{q(N(\mathfrak{q}))^{n-1}}\},

and the association
g\mapsto g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}=b=\alpha+b_{-}
yields an identification between
\Hom_{W/\penalty\pi^{n}}(\underline{P}^{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{n}},\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}})
and
\End_{W/\penalty\pi^{n}}(\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}})\cdot\mathfrak{a}
in
B
such that
\deg(g)=N(b)/\penalty N(\mathfrak{a})
. Under this identification, we have
[
35
, Prop. 5.3]
10
10

10

The function
H_{k-t-1,t}(x)
in
[
35
]
is equal to the function
P_{k,t}(x)
defined in (
11
).

\left(\bar{\epsilon}\left(X^{\otimes k-t-1}_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}\otimes X^{\otimes 2t}_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}\right),\epsilon\left(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}\right)\right)=\frac{(4D)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2N(b_{-})}{N(b)}\right).

Combining this with Proposition
12.2
yields

(a(Y),T_{m}\bar{a}(Y^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{v}=\frac{|S|}{2}m^{k-t-1}\frac{(4D)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=\alpha+b_{-}\in R\mathfrak{a}\\
\begin{subarray}{c}N(b)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\\
|D|N(b_{-})\equiv 0\mod{q(N(\mathfrak{q}))^{n-1}}\end{subarray}\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2N(b_{-})}{N(b)}\right).

The result follows after interchanging the order of summation in the double sum and noting that

\#\{n\geq 1\colon|D|N(b_{-})\equiv 0\mod q(N(\mathfrak{q})^{n-1})\}=U(b_{-}).

∎

Let
\langle\;,\;\rangle^{\GS}_{\mathrm{fin}}=\sum_{v\nmid\infty}\langle\;,\;\rangle_{v}\epsilon_{v}
be the sum of the local heights on
X
over all the finite places of
H
.

Proposition 12.4
.

Assume that
(m,N)=1
and
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
. Then

\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}=-u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|m)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{0<n<\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right).

Proof.

The only difference between the formula of Proposition
12.3
and the one of
[
31
, II Prop. 4.15]
is the fact that the sum is weighted by
\bar{\alpha}^{2t}
. The factor responsible for the appearance of
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m|D|-nN)
in
[
31
]
is thus replaced by

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{c}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}\\
\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathfrak{c}]=\mathcal{A}^{-1}\\
N(\mathfrak{c})=m|D|-nN\end{subarray}\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t},

where
\mathfrak{c}=(\alpha)\mathfrak{d}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
and
\alpha\in\mathfrak{d}^{-1}\mathfrak{a}
. But this expression is equal to

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{c}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}\\
\begin{subarray}{c}[\mathfrak{c}]=\mathcal{A}^{-1}\\
N(\mathfrak{c})=m|D|-nN\end{subarray}\end{subarray}}\bar{\chi}(\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})=\frac{\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})}{D^{t}}r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\chi}}(m|D|-nN)=\frac{\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})}{D^{t}}r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN).

∎

12.2.
The case
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)\neq 0

Given points
\underline{x}_{1}
and
\underline{x}_{2}
of
\underline{X}_{0}(N)
, reduction of homomorphisms induces injections

\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n+1}}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2})\hookrightarrow\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2}),\qquad n\geq 1

and we have

\Hom_{L^{\mathrm{ur}}}(x_{1},x_{2})=\Hom_{W}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2})=\bigcap_{n\geq 1}\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2}).

We define

\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}^{\mathrm{new}}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2}):=\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2})\setminus\Hom_{W}(\underline{x}_{1},\underline{x}_{2}).

The integer
2ur_{\mathcal{A}}(m)
is the multiplicity with which
P_{\mathfrak{n}}
occurs in
T_{m}P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
, i.e.,

|\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{P}_{\mathfrak{n}})_{m}|=2ur_{\mathcal{A}}(m).

Theorem 12.5
.

Assume that
(m,N)=1
. Then

\displaystyle\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}

\displaystyle=-u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|m)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{0<n<\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{|S|(4|D|m)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}hur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\log\frac{N}{m}.

Proof.

We use the same notations as in the proof of Proposition
12.2
. As in the proof of Proposition
12.2
, we have

(89)

(\underline{x},T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{v}=|S|m^{k-t-1}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})_{m_{0}}}(\underline{y}\cdot T_{q^{s}}\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma})(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{hg\sqrt{D}g^{-1}h^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{hg\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t})),

where
h
is an arbitrary element of
\Hom_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}
for some
n\geq 1
. Note that this element
h
can be the reduction of an element of
\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}
in the present case since
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)\neq 0
.

We begin by assuming that
v
sits over a prime
q
that is non-split in
K
. In particular,
q
does not divide
N
.
By
[
22
, III (8.5)]
, we have

(\underline{y}\cdot T_{q^{s}}\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Hom^{\mathrm{new}}_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}}1+\frac{v_{q}}{2}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}}1,

with
v_{q}=s
or
s/2
depending on whether
q
is ramified or inert in
K
.
Injecting this into the above expression, we obtain

\displaystyle(\underline{x},T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{g\in\Hom^{\mathrm{new}}_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))

\displaystyle\qquad+v_{q}\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t})),

where the equality holds because (
88
) is true for arbitrary
h
.

The quaternionic expression of Proposition
12.3
continues to hold for the first term if we impose the additional constraint that
b_{-}\neq 0
(always satisfied when
r_{\mathcal{A}}(m)=0
):

\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{g\in\Hom^{\mathrm{new}}_{\Lambda/\pi^{n}}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))\\
=|S|m^{k-t-1}\frac{(4D)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\begin{subarray}{c}b=\alpha+b_{-}\in R\mathfrak{a}/\penalty\pm 1\\
N(b)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}\\
b_{-}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}U(b_{-})P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2N(b_{-})}{N(b)}\right).

It remains to handle the second term above, namely

v_{q}\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t})).

The result
[
35
, Prop. 5.3]
continues to hold in this case with
b_{-}=0
since
g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}=b=\alpha+b_{-}\in\End_{\Lambda}(\underline{y})=\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Hence, we obtain

(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))=\frac{(4D)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}P_{k,t}(1).

But
P_{k,t}(1)=1
, and we deduce that

v_{q}\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))\\
=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{2\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}\\
g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}=\alpha\in\End_{\Lambda}(\underline{y})\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}.

Recall that
y=P_{\mathfrak{n}}
is represented by
A=\mathbb{C}/\mathcal{O}_{K}
,
y^{\sigma}=P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}
is represented by
A^{\mathfrak{a}}=\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
, and with these identifications the isogeny
\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}
is the quotient isogeny
\mathbb{C}/\mathcal{O}_{K}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
. In particular,
g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}\in\End(\mathbb{C}/\mathcal{O}_{K})
is given by multiplication by some
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
that must satisfy
\alpha a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
for all
a\in\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
. In other words, we must have
\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}
. The norm of
\alpha
is
\deg(g)\deg(\phi_{\mathfrak{a}})=mN(\mathfrak{a})
. We have shown that the assignment
g\mapsto g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}
gives a map

\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}{\longrightarrow\,}\{\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}\colon N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\}.

This map is injective and
\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}
has size
2ur_{\mathcal{A}}(m)
. On the other hand, the integral ideal
\alpha\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\in\mathcal{A}^{-1}
has norm
m
and thus the right hand set has size
|\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}|r_{\mathcal{A}^{-1}}(m)=2ur_{\mathcal{A}}(m)
. We deduce that the map is a bijection. We thus obtain

\displaystyle v_{q}\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}

\displaystyle(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))

\displaystyle=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{2\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}\\
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}

\displaystyle=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{2\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}\\
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}}\bar{\chi}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{2\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\bar{\chi}(\mathfrak{a}^{-1})^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}\\
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\alpha)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}}\bar{\chi}(\alpha\mathfrak{a}^{-1})

\displaystyle=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})u\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{c}\in\mathcal{A}^{-1}\\
N(\mathfrak{c})=m\end{subarray}}\bar{\chi}(\mathfrak{c})

\displaystyle=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})ur_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\chi}}(m)

\displaystyle=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m).

To summarize, when
v
sits over a non-split prime
q
, we have established that

\displaystyle(\underline{x},T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{v}

\displaystyle=|S|m^{k-t-1}\frac{(4D)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\begin{subarray}{c}b=\alpha+b_{-}\in R\mathfrak{a}/\penalty\pm 1\\
N(b)=mN(\mathfrak{a})\end{subarray}\\
b_{-}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\bar{\alpha}^{2t}U(b_{-})P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2N(b_{-})}{N(b)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m).

Next, we deal with the case when
v
sits above a prime
q
that splits in
K
, say
q\mathcal{O}_{K}=\mathfrak{q}\bar{\mathfrak{q}}
. Our starting point is (
89
). In this case, by
[
22
, III (8.5) 3) and III (8.6)]
, we have

(\underline{y}\cdot T_{q^{s}}\underline{y}^{\sigma}_{g})=\frac{v_{q}}{2}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}_{g}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{q^{s}}}1,

where

v_{q}=\begin{cases}k_{\mathfrak{q}}&\text{ if }v\nmid N,\text{ where }k_{\mathfrak{q}}\geq 0\text{ and }k_{\mathfrak{q}}+k_{\bar{\mathfrak{q}}}=s\\
0&\text{ if }v\mid\mathfrak{n}\\
-\ord_{q}(N)&\text{ if }v\mid\bar{\mathfrak{n}}.\end{cases}

Injecting this expression into (
89
) yields

(\underline{x},T_{m}\overline{\underline{x}}^{\mathfrak{a}})_{v}=v_{q}\frac{|S|m^{k-t-1}}{2}\sum_{g\in\Hom_{\Lambda}(\underline{y}^{\sigma},\underline{y})_{m}}(\bar{\epsilon}(X_{g\sqrt{D}g^{-1}}^{\otimes k-t-1}\otimes X_{\overline{g\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}}^{\otimes 2t}),\epsilon(X_{\sqrt{D}}^{k-t-1}\otimes X_{1}^{\otimes 2t}))\\
=v_{q}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\chi(\bar{\mathfrak{a}})ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m).

(This exact expression was calculated above.)
Putting everything together, we deduce that

\displaystyle\langle Z,

\displaystyle T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}

\displaystyle=-u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|m)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{0<n<\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(-1)^{k+t}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\sum_{q}v_{q}\sum_{v\mid q}\log N(v)

\displaystyle=-u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|m)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{0<n<\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(-1)^{k+t}\frac{|S|(4mD)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}ur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\left(\sum_{q\text{ split}}h\ord_{q}(m/N)\log q+\sum_{q\text{ non-split}}h\ord_{q}(m)\log q\right)

\displaystyle=-u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|m)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{0<n<\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{|S|(4m|D|)^{k-t-1}}{\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}hur_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\log(N/m).

∎

13.
Final formula

We recall from (
27
) and (
28
) that

Z_{\mathcal{A}}=\chi(\mathfrak{a})^{-1}Z_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}=\bar{\chi}(\mathfrak{a})^{-1}\bar{Z}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}.

Define

\Delta:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\deg(\pi_{M,N})}}(Z+\bar{Z}).

If
\mathcal{A}=[\mathfrak{a}]\in\Cl_{K}
, then define

\Delta_{\mathcal{A},\chi}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\deg(\pi_{M,N})}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}}+\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}).

Recall from (
29
) the cycle

\Delta_{\chi}:=\sum_{\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}}\Delta_{\mathcal{A},\chi}\in\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0,K(\sqrt{\deg(\pi_{M,N})})(\chi)}.

Recall that
g_{\mathcal{A}}=\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{m}(\mathcal{A})q^{n}\in S_{2k}^{\new}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
represents the linear functional on
S_{2k}^{\new}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
given by

f\longmapsto\frac{(2k-2)!\sqrt{|D|}D^{t}}{2^{4k-1}\pi^{2k}}L^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}(f,\chi,k+t).

Theorem 13.1
.

Assume that
0<t\leq k-1
. For all
(m,N)=1
, we have

\langle\Delta_{\chi},T_{m}\Delta_{\chi}\rangle^{\GS}=2hu^{2}\frac{(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\mathcal{A}}a_{m}(\mathcal{A}).

Proof.

By Theorem
3.6
, for any
(m,N)=1
, we have

a_{m}(\mathcal{A})=a_{m}^{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{A})+a_{m}^{\infty}(\mathcal{A}),

where

(90)

\displaystyle a_{m}^{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=m^{k-t-1}\left[\frac{h}{u}D^{t}r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m)\log\frac{N}{m}-\sum_{0<n\leq\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\right]

(91)

\displaystyle a_{m}^{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=m^{k-t-1}\left[\frac{h}{u}D^{t}r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m)\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k+t)+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\Gamma}(k-t)+\log\left(\frac{|D|}{4\pi^{2}}\right)+2\frac{L^{\prime}}{L}(1,\epsilon_{K})\right)\right.

(92)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}(n)r_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\chi}(m|D|+nN)Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\right].

By Theorem
12.5
, using also (
86
), we have

(94)

\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}=\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}=u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}a_{m}^{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{A}).

By Theorem
11.3
, we have

(95)

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}=u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}a_{m}^{\infty}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}).

Recall that
|S|=\deg(\pi_{M,N})
.
We deduce that, for all
(m,N=1)
, we have

\displaystyle h^{-1}\langle\Delta_{\chi},T_{m}\Delta_{\chi}\rangle^{\GS}

\displaystyle=\langle\Delta,T_{m}\sum_{\mathcal{A}}\Delta_{\mathcal{A},\chi}\rangle^{\GS}

\displaystyle=\langle Z+\bar{Z},T_{m}\sum_{\mathcal{A}}(Z_{\mathcal{A}}+\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}})\rangle^{\GS}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathcal{A}}(\langle Z,T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}+\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\GS}+\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS}+\langle\bar{Z},T_{m}\bar{Z}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\rangle_{\infty}^{\GS})

\displaystyle=u^{2}\frac{(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\mathcal{A}}(2a_{m}^{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathcal{A})+2a_{m}^{\infty}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}))

\displaystyle=2u^{2}\frac{(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{\mathcal{A}}a_{m}(\mathcal{A}).

∎

Define
W
to be the subspace of generalized Heegner cycles generated by

\{T_{m}\Delta_{\chi}\mid(m,N)=1\}.

Let
W^{\prime}
denote the quotient of
W
modulo the null space with respect to the Gillet–Soulé height pairing on
W\times W
.

Proposition 13.2
.

The module
W^{\prime}
is isomorphic as a Hecke module to a subquotient of
S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
.

Proof.

The argument is the same as
[
41
, Prop. 5.1.2]
, but requires Lemma
13.3
, which we prove below.
∎

Lemma 13.3
.

For
n
and
m
coprime to
N
, we have

T_{n}T_{m}\Delta_{\chi}=\sum_{d\mid(n,m)}d^{2k-1}T_{\frac{nm}{d^{2}}}\Delta_{\chi}.

Proof.

We immediately reduce to the case where
m
and
n
are powers of a single prime
p
. Since the action of Hecke operators on
\Delta_{\chi}
lies over the action of the Hecke operators on the modular curve, the desired formula follows from the case
k=1
and the formula
f^{*}\Delta_{\chi}=p^{2k-2}\Delta_{\chi}
, where
f=[p]\times 1\colon W_{2k-2}\times A^{2t}\to W_{2k-2}\times A^{2t}
. To verify this, we consider separately, in the definition of the generalized Heegner cycle (see Section
5.2
), the
k-t-1
pure Kuga–Sato factors and the
2t
“mixed” factors. Let
\eta
be the projection onto
H^{1}(E_{y})^{\otimes 2}
, so that, e.g.,
\eta\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}-E_{y}\times\{0\}-\{0\}\times E_{y}
. On the pure Kuga–Sato factors, the pullback of
\eta\Gamma_{\sqrt{D}}
along
[p]\times[p]\colon E_{y}\times E_{y}\to E_{y}\times E_{y}
is
p^{2}\eta\Gamma_{\sqrt{D}}

[
41
, 2.4.2]
. On the mixed factors the pullback of
\eta\Gamma_{1}
along
[p]\times[1]\colon E_{y}\times A_{F}\to E_{y}\times A_{F}
is
\eta\Gamma_{p}=p\eta\Gamma_{1}
. It follows that
f^{*}\epsilon Y=p^{2k-2t-2}p^{2t}\epsilon Y=p^{2k-2}\epsilon Y
, and hence
f^{*}\Delta_{\chi}=p^{2k-2}\Delta_{\chi}
, as claimed.
∎

Given an element
T\in W
, denote its image in
W^{\prime}
by
T^{\prime}
. If
f
is a normalized newform, we may consider the
f
-isotypic component of
\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi}
, which we denote by
\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f}
.

Theorem 13.4
.

Assume that
0<t\leq k-1
. For any normalized newform
f\in S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
, we have

L^{\prime}(f,\chi,k+t)=\frac{4^{k+t}\pi^{2k}(f,f)}{(k-t-1)!(k+t-1)!hu^{2}\sqrt{|D|}|D|^{k-t-1}}\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f},\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f}\rangle^{\GS}.

Proof.

We have

L^{\prime}(f,\chi,k+t)=\sum_{\mathcal{A}}L_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(f,\chi,k+t)=\frac{2^{4k-1}\pi^{2k}}{(2k-2)!\sqrt{|D|}D^{t}}(f,\sum_{\mathcal{A}}g_{\mathcal{A}}).

Extend
\{f\}
to a basis
\{f_{1}=f,f_{2},\ldots,f_{d}\}
of
S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
consisting of the normalized newforms together with a basis of the space of oldforms. We may then write

\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}},

such that
T_{m}\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}}=a_{m}(f_{i})\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}}
for all
(m,N)=1
. We then have

\langle\Delta_{\chi},T_{m}\Delta_{\chi}\rangle^{\GS}=\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi},T_{m}\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi}\rangle^{\GS}=\sum_{i,j}\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}},T_{m}\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{j}}\rangle^{\GS}=\sum_{i,j}a_{m}(f_{j})\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}},\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{j}}\rangle^{\GS}.

Using Theorem
13.1
, we deduce that up to oldforms we have

\sum_{\mathcal{A}}g_{\mathcal{A}}=\frac{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}{2u^{2}h(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}\sum_{i,j}\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}},\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{j}}\rangle^{\GS}f_{j}.

The cycles
\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{j}}
have different eigenvalues with respect to the Hecke operators, which are self-dual with respect to the height pairing. Thus,
\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{i}},\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f_{j}}\rangle^{\GS}=0
if
i\neq j
and

L^{\prime}(f,\chi,k+t)=\frac{2^{4k-1}\pi^{2k}}{(2k-2)!\sqrt{|D|}D^{t}}\frac{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}{2u^{2}h(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}\overline{\langle\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f},\Delta^{\prime}_{\chi,f}\rangle^{\GS}}.

The result follows since
\langle\;,\;\rangle^{\GS}
is Hermitian and in particular, any self-pairing is a real number.
∎

14.
Algebraicity

We prove generalizations of the algebraicity results of Gross–Zagier
[
22
, V (4.3)]
and Zhang
[
41
, Thm. 5.2.2]
. Define

(96)

\mathcal{G}^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(z,z^{\prime}):=\frac{|S|(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in R_{N}^{m}/\pm 1\\
\gamma z^{\prime}\neq z\end{subarray}}g_{k,t}(z,\gamma z^{\prime})\alpha(\gamma,z,z^{\prime})^{2t}\\
+uD^{t}r_{\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2},\chi}(m)\lim\limits_{w\to z}(g_{k,t}(z,w)-\log|2\pi i\eta^{4}(z)(w-z)|_{v})\bigg],

where we recall that for
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in R_{N}^{m}
, we have
\alpha(\gamma,z,z^{\prime})=c\bar{z}z^{\prime}+d\bar{z}-az^{\prime}-b
, and

g_{k,t}(z,z^{\prime}):=-Q_{k,t}\left(1+\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{2yy^{\prime}}\right),

with
Q_{k,t}
the Jacobi function of the second type (
17
).

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}
with corresponding Galois element
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
. Let
\tau_{0}=\frac{-1+\sqrt{D}}{2}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{0}=P_{\mathfrak{n}}
, as in Section
10.2
.
Define the quantity

\gamma^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(\mathcal{A}):=\sum_{\rho\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)}\mathcal{G}^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(\tau_{0}^{\rho},\tau_{0}^{\rho\sigma})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\Cl_{K}\\
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{G}^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}},\tau_{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}).

By Theorem
11.9
and (
95
), we have

\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\rangle^{\GS}_{\infty}=m^{k-t-1}\gamma^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(\mathcal{A})=u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}a_{m}^{\infty}(\bar{\mathcal{A}}),

where
a_{m}^{\infty}(\bar{\mathcal{A}})
is given by (
91
). We deduce that

(97)

a_{m}(\bar{\mathcal{A}})=\frac{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}{u^{2}|S|(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}m^{k-t-1}\gamma^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(\mathcal{A})\\
+m^{k-t-1}\left[\frac{h}{u}D^{t}r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\log\frac{N}{m}-\sum_{0<n\leq\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma^{\prime}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\right].

Let
\lambda=\{\lambda_{m}\}_{\geq 1}
be a relation for
S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
, in the sense of
[
22
, p. 316]
, so that:

i)

\lambda_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\lambda_{m}=0
for all but finitely many
m
;

ii)

\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}a_{m}=0
for all
\sum_{m\geq 1}a_{m}q^{m}\in S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
;

iii)

\lambda_{m}=0
for
(m,N)\neq 1
.

Define

\gamma_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\mathcal{A}):=\sum_{m\geq 1}m^{k-t-1}\lambda_{m}\gamma_{M,N,k,t,\chi}^{m}(\mathcal{A})=\sum_{\rho\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)}\mathcal{G}_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\tau_{0}^{\rho},\tau_{0}^{\rho\sigma}).

Then (
97
) implies the equality:

Theorem 14.1
.

For all
0<t\leq k-1
,
\lambda
a relation for
S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
, and
\mathcal{A}
an ideal class of
K
, we have

(98)

\gamma_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\mathcal{A})=u^{2}\frac{|S|(4|D|)^{k-t-1}}{D^{t}\binom{2k-2}{k-t-1}}\sum_{m\geq 1}m^{k-t-1}\lambda_{m}\bigg[-\frac{h}{u}D^{t}r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m)\log\frac{N}{m}\\
+\sum_{0<n\leq\frac{m|D|}{N}}\sigma^{\prime}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)r_{\mathcal{A},\chi}(m|D|-nN)P_{k,t}\left(1-\frac{2nN}{m|D|}\right)\bigg].

The quantity
\sigma^{\prime}_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}}(n)
appearing in the sum on the right hand side is a non-negative even integral multiple of the logarithm of a prime (by the remark following
[
22
, IV (4.6)]
). Hence, Theorem
14.1
expresses
\gamma_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\mathcal{A})
as a
\mathbb{Q}(\chi)
-rational linear combination of logarithms of primes,
generalizing
[
22
, V (4.3)]
to the case
t>0
.

We also obtain the following result in support of a generalization of the algebraicity conjecture of Gross–Zagier
[
22
, V (4.4)]
:

Theorem 14.2
.

Let
0<t\leq k-1
,
\lambda
be a relation for
S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
, and
\sigma\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
.
Assume that the height pairing on generalized Heegner cycles is non-degenerate. Then there exists a
K(\chi)
-rational number
c
and an element
