\delta\in H\otimes K(\chi)
such that

\mathcal{G}_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\tau_{0}^{\rho},\tau_{0}^{\rho\sigma})=c\log|\delta^{\rho}|_{v},

for all
\rho\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
, where
v\colon H\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the embedding fixed in Section
10.2
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the ideal class corresponding to
\sigma
.
By Theorem
11.8
and (
76
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\tau_{0}^{\rho},\tau_{0}^{\rho\sigma})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\geq 1}m^{k-t-1}\lambda_{m}\mathcal{G}^{m}_{M,N,k,t,\chi}(\tau_{0}^{\rho},\tau_{0}^{\rho\sigma})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}\left\langle Z_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}\right\rangle_{v}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}\left\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\right\rangle_{w}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}T_{m}Z,Z_{\mathcal{A}}\right\rangle_{w},

where
\mathcal{A}_{1}
is the ideal class corresponding to
\rho\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
,
\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathcal{A}
, and
w
is the infinite place corresponding to
\rho
. Thus, the individual terms
\mathcal{G}_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\tau_{0}^{\rho},\tau_{0}^{\rho\sigma})
in the definition of
\gamma_{M,N,k,t,\chi,\lambda}(\mathcal{A})
are the local height pairings at archimedean places of the cycles
\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}T_{m}Z
and
Z_{\mathcal{A}}
. Using the non-degeneracy of the height pairing and Proposition
13.2
, the fact that
\lambda
is a relation for
S_{2k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
implies that
\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}T_{m}Z=0\in\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0,K(\chi)}
. Thus, there exist subvarieties
W_{i}
of
X
and functions
f_{i}
in
W_{i}
defined over
H
such that

\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}T_{m}Z=c\sum_{i}\mathrm{div}(f_{i}|W_{i}),

for some
c\in K(\chi)
. It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 1}\lambda_{m}\left\langle Z,T_{m}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\right\rangle_{w}

\displaystyle=c\sum_{i}\log|f_{i}(Z_{\mathcal{A}}\cdot W_{i})|w

\displaystyle=c\log\bigg|\prod_{i}f_{i}(Z_{\mathcal{A}}\cdot W_{i})\bigg|_{w}

\displaystyle=c\log\bigg|\bigg(\prod_{i}f_{i}(Z_{\mathcal{A}}\cdot W_{i})\bigg)^{\rho}\bigg|_{v}.

∎

Theorem
14.2
generalizes
[
41
, Thm. 5.2.2]
to the case
t>0
.

15.
Corollary

Proof of Corollary
1.4
.

Fix a prime
\ell\nmid 2(2k-1)!N\varphi(N)
split in
K
.
Let
\chi^{\prime}:=\chi\cdot\mathrm{Nm}^{-t}
, a Hecke character of infinity type
(t,-t)
.
Consider the
\ell
-adic
{\rm Gal}_{K}
-representation
V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell}=V_{f,\ell}(k)\otimes\chi_{\ell}^{\prime}=V_{f,\ell}(k)\otimes\chi_{\ell}(t)
.
The Rankin–Selberg
L
-function
L(f,\chi,s)
has global root number
-1
.

Let
F/\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
be a finite extension containing the Fourier coefficients of
F
and the values of
\chi^{\prime}
. Consider the generalized Kuga–Sato variety
X^{\prime}:=W_{2k-2}(\Gamma_{1}(N))\times A^{2k-2}
, where
W_{2k-2}(\Gamma_{1}(N))
is the Kuga–Sato
(2k-1)
-fold over the modular curve
X_{1}(N)
with
\Gamma_{1}(N)
-level structure, and
A
is a CM elliptic curve as in
[
13
, §4.1]
. It contains the generalized Heegner cycle

\Upsilon:=\epsilon_{W}\otimes\epsilon_{A}((\Gamma_{\id}^{T})^{2k-2})\in\CH^{2k-1}(X^{\prime}_{H})_{0},

where the projectors
\epsilon_{W}
and
\epsilon_{A}
are the ones defined in
[
3
, (2.1.2), (1.4.4)]
. Define a class
z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\in H_{f}^{1}(K,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell})
by

z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}=\mathrm{cor}_{H/K}(z_{f,\chi^{\prime},1}),

where
z_{f,\chi^{\prime},1}\in H^{1}(H,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell})
is the
\chi_{\ell}^{\prime}
-component of the image of
\AJ_{X^{\prime}}^{2k-1}(\Upsilon)
in

H^{1}(H,V_{f,\ell}(k)\otimes\sym^{2k-2}H^{1}_{\et}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(1))).

By
[
13
, Thm. B]
, we have

(99)

z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0\implies H^{1}_{f}(K,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell})=F\cdot z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}.

Since we assume the injectivity of
\AJ^{k+t}_{X}
, to prove Corollary
1.4
it suffices to prove that

\ord_{s=1}L(f,\chi,s)=1\implies z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0.

Let
\tilde{X}:=W_{2k-2}(\Gamma_{1}(N))\times A^{2t}
and define the generalized Heegner cycles

\Upsilon^{t}_{\mathcal{A}}=\epsilon_{W}\otimes\epsilon_{A}((\Gamma_{\sqrt{D}})^{k-t-1}\times(\Gamma_{\phi_{\mathfrak{a}}}^{T})^{2t})\in\CH^{k+t}(\tilde{X}_{H})_{0}.

Define

z^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}=\mathrm{cor}_{H/K}(z^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime},1})\in H^{1}(K,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell}),

where
z^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime},1}\in H^{1}(H,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell})
is the
\chi_{\ell}^{\prime}
-component of the image of of
\AJ_{\tilde{X}}^{k+t}(\Upsilon^{t}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}]})
in

H^{1}(H,V_{f,\ell}(k)\otimes\sym^{2t}H^{1}_{\et}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(1))).

By
[
5
, Prop. 4.1.1]
, there exists an algebraic correspondence mapping
\Upsilon
to an integral multiple of
\Upsilon^{t}_{[\mathcal{O}]_{K}}
. Thus, in order to prove that
z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0
, it suffices to prove that
z^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0
.

Recall the generalized Kuga–Sato variety
X=W_{2k-2}\times A^{2t}
studied in this paper, along with the generalized Heegner cycles
Z_{\mathcal{A}},\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\in\CH^{k+t}(X_{H})_{0,K(\chi)}
.
Define
\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\in H^{1}(K,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell})
to be the
\chi^{\prime}
-component of

\mathrm{cor}_{H/K}(\AJ_{X,f}^{k+t}(Z))\in H^{1}(K,V_{f,\ell}(k)\otimes\kappa_{2t}H_{\et}^{2t}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(t))),

where
\AJ^{k+t}_{X,f}
denotes the composition

\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0}\overset{\AJ^{k+t}_{X}}{{\longrightarrow\,}}H^{1}(H,H_{\et}^{2k-1}(\bar{X},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(k))\otimes\kappa_{2t}H_{\et}^{2t}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(t)))\overset{\epsilon_{f}}{{\longrightarrow\,}}H^{1}(K,V_{f,\ell}(k)\otimes\kappa_{2t}H_{\et}^{2t}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(t))).

Note that
\kappa_{2t}H^{2t}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(t))
is a quotient of
\epsilon_{A}H^{2t}(\bar{A},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(t))
. In order to prove that
z^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0
, it therefore suffices to prove that
\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0
(the different level structures have no effect since we are projecting the cohomology classes to the cohomology of the representation
V_{f,\ell}
, which can be cut out from the cohomology of
W_{2k-2}
and/or
W_{2k-2}(\Gamma_{1}(N))
).

We have

\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}=\sum_{\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}}\AJ_{X,f}^{k+t}(Z_{\mathcal{A}})=\AJ_{X,f}^{k+t}\bigg(\sum_{\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}}Z_{\mathcal{A}}\bigg).

Let
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(H/,\mathbb{Q})
be a lift of a generator of
{\rm Gal}(K/\mathbb{Q})
. Let
(-1)^{k}\nu_{f}
be the sign of the functional equation of
L(f,s)
. By
[
35
, Lem. 4.8]
, we have

\tau(\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}})=(-1)^{k-t-1}\nu_{f}\chi^{\prime}(\mathfrak{n})\AJ_{X,f}^{k+t}\bigg(\sum_{\mathcal{A}\in\Cl_{K}}\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}\bigg).

In view of (
29
), we thus have

(100)

\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}+(-1)^{k-t-1}\nu_{f}\chi^{\prime}(\bar{\mathfrak{n}})\tau(\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}})=\sqrt{\deg(\pi_{M,N})}\AJ^{k+t}_{X}(\Delta_{\chi,f}).

Using Theorem
1.1
, we see that

\displaystyle\ord_{s=k+t}L(f,\chi,s)=1

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{thm:intro}}{\implies}\langle\Delta_{\chi,f},\Delta_{\chi,f}\rangle^{\GS}\neq 0

\displaystyle\implies\Delta_{\chi,f}\neq 0

\displaystyle\overset{\AJ\text{ inj.}}{\implies}\AJ_{X}^{k+t}(\Delta_{\chi,f})\neq 0

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{zchi}}{\implies}\tilde{z}^{t}_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0

\displaystyle\implies z_{f,\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ThmB}}{\implies}\dim_{F}H^{1}_{f}(K,V_{f,\chi^{\prime},\ell})=1

\displaystyle\overset{\AJ\text{ inj.}}{\implies}\dim_{K(\chi)}\epsilon_{f,\chi}\CH^{k+t}(X)_{0}=1.

∎

Appendix: Conventions for Heegner points

Following
[
20
]
, any Heegner point on
X_{0}(N)
for
K
can be described as
(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}):=(\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1})
for any integral ideal
\mathfrak{a}
such that
[\mathfrak{a}]=\mathcal{A}
. The identification of
Y_{0}(N)
with
\Gamma_{0}(N)\backslash\mathcal{H}
is obtained as follows. Given a point
\tau\in\mathcal{H}
, assign to it
(\mathbb{C}/\langle 1,\tau\rangle,1/N+\langle 1,\tau\rangle)
. Conversely, given a cyclic
N
-isogeny
E{\longrightarrow\,}E^{\prime}
, write
E=\mathbb{C}/L
and
E^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}/L^{\prime}
. Up to homothety, we may assume
L\subset L^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{C}/L{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/L^{\prime}
is the identity map on covering spaces. We can then choose an oriented basis
L=\langle\omega_{2},\omega_{1}\rangle
such that
\tau=\omega_{1}/\omega_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
and
L^{\prime}=\langle\omega_{2}/N,\omega_{1}\rangle
. The point
(E{\longrightarrow\,}E^{\prime})
of
Y_{0}(N)
then corresponds to
\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau
. The point corresponding to the Heegner point
(\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n})
will be denoted by
z_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}\in\mathcal{H}
. Since
z_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}\in K
and
Nz_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}\in K
, the point satisfies a quadratic equation
az_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}^{2}+bz_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}+c=0
with
\gcd(a,b,c)=1
,
D=b^{2}-4ac
, and
N\mid a
. We have
\bar{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}=z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\mathfrak{n}}}
. If
\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}\in{\rm Gal}(H/K)
corresponds to
{\mathcal{B}}\in\Cl_{K}
under the Artin map, then
z_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}^{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}}=z_{\mathcal{A}{\mathcal{B}}^{-1},\mathfrak{n}}
. Letting
p\mid N
,
p\mathcal{O}_{K}=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{p}^{k}\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{k}\mathfrak{m}
, we have
w_{p}(z_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}})=z_{\mathcal{A}[\bar{p}^{k}],\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
. These are the conventions used in
[
31
, p. 663]
.

These conventions differ slightly from the ones in
[
22
]
. We begin by recalling that
\mathcal{O}_{K}=\langle 1,\frac{-1+\sqrt{D}}{2}\rangle
. The ideal
\mathfrak{n}
is determined by some
\beta\in\mathbb{Z}/2N\mathbb{Z}
such that
\beta^{2}\equiv D\pmod{4N}
and
\mathfrak{n}=\langle N,\frac{\beta+\sqrt{D}}{2}\rangle
. We have
N(\mathfrak{n})=N
and
\mathfrak{n}^{-1}=\bar{\mathfrak{n}}N^{-1}=\langle 1,\frac{-\beta+\sqrt{D}}{2N}\rangle
. Consider a quadratic equation of the form

(101)

AX^{2}+BX+C=0,\quad A>0,\quad B^{2}-4AC=D,\quad N\mid A,\quad B\equiv\beta\pmod{2N},

and consider the integral ideal
\mathfrak{a}=\langle A,\frac{B+\sqrt{D}}{2}\rangle
. Observe that
N(\mathfrak{a})=A
,
\mathfrak{a}^{-1}=\bar{\mathfrak{a}}A^{-1}=\langle 1,\frac{-B+\sqrt{D}}{2A}\rangle
, and
\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1}=\langle\frac{A}{N},\frac{B+\sqrt{D}}{2}\rangle
. Gross and Zagier define
\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}=(-B+\sqrt{D})/2A\in\mathcal{H}
to be the solution to (
101
) that lies in
\mathcal{H}
. Then
\mathfrak{a}^{-1}=\langle 1,\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}\rangle
. Observe moreover that

\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\bar{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}=\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}N^{-1}=(\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1})^{-1}N^{-1}=\overline{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1}}NA^{-1}N^{-1}=\overline{\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{n}^{-1}}A^{-1}=\left\langle\frac{1}{N},\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}\right\rangle.

We deduce that

\Gamma_{0}(N)\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\mathfrak{n}}}=\Gamma_{0}(N)\bar{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}.

In particular, we obtain

(102)

\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}^{\sigma_{{\mathcal{B}}}}=z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\mathfrak{n}}}^{\sigma_{{\mathcal{B}}}}=z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}{\mathcal{B}}^{-1},\bar{\mathfrak{n}}}=\tau_{\mathcal{A}{\mathcal{B}},\mathfrak{n}}.

This is coherent with the statement
[
22
, p. 235]
that “
{\rm Gal}(H/K)\simeq\Cl_{K}
acts by multiplication on
\mathcal{A}
”. Letting
p\mid N
,
p\mathcal{O}_{K}=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{p}^{k}\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}=\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{k}\mathfrak{m}
, we have

(103)

w_{p}(\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}})=w_{p}(z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\bar{\mathfrak{n}}})=z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}}[\mathfrak{p}^{k}],\mathfrak{p}^{k}\bar{\mathfrak{m}}}=\tau_{\mathcal{A}[\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{k}],\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{k}\mathfrak{m}}=\tau_{\mathcal{A}[\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{k}],\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}.

Note that the cycles
Z_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\bar{Z}_{\mathcal{A}}
sit above the Heegner point

P_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{a}}=(\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}{\longrightarrow\,}\mathbb{C}/\mathfrak{a}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{-1})=z_{\bar{\mathcal{A}},\mathfrak{n}}=\tau_{\mathcal{A},\bar{\mathfrak{n}}}=(A^{\mathfrak{a}}{\longrightarrow\,}A^{\mathfrak{a}}/A^{\mathfrak{a}}[\mathfrak{n}]).

Equation (
103
) is consistent with the claim
[
22
, p. 243]
about Atkin–Lehner, but inconsistent with the claim in
[
22
, p. 236 i)]
. It is moreover claimed in
[
22
, top of p. 243]
that
\tau_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}}^{\sigma_{{\mathcal{B}}}}=\tau_{\mathcal{A}{\mathcal{B}}^{-1},\mathfrak{n}}
. This contradicts (
102
) and is the reason that all sums in the archimedean calculation of
[
22
]
are over ideal classes
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
satisfying
\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{-1}=\mathcal{A}
, when the sums should in fact be taken over ideal classes
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
satisfying
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}
. This does not affect the results of
[
22
]
because there is no infinite order Hecke character to deal with, but it does matter in our setting. In the same vein, it seems that
[
41
, 2nd equality bottom p. 142]
should contain
\mathcal{A}^{-1}
instead of
\mathcal{A}
. Once again, this does not affect the results of
[
41
]
, since there is no infinite order Hecke character.
